================================================================================
  NORTH AFRICAN / ALGERIAN TRADITIONAL FOOD — DRUG INTERACTION DATASET
  Format: Whole Food | Main Components | Drugs (Possible Interactions)
  [✓] = Component confirmed present in unified-DFI food_id_map
================================================================================

─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 #  | WHOLE FOOD               | MAIN COMPONENTS              | INTERACTING DRUGS
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

01 | Couscous                  | Phytic Acid (IP6) [✓]        | Levothyroxine (Thyroid)
   | (Kosksi / Barboucha /    | SMILES: OP(O)(=O)OC1C(OP    | Ciprofloxacin (Quinolones)
   | Ta'am)                   | (O)(O)=O)C(OP(O)(O)=O)C     |
   |                           | (OP(O)(O)=O)C(OP(O)(O)=O)   |
   |                           | C1OP(O)(O)=O                 |
   |                           |------------------------------|
   |                           | Ferulic Acid                 | Digoxin (Heart)
   |                           | SMILES: COC1=C(C=CC(=C1)    |
   |                           | /C=C/C(=O)O)O                |
   |                           |                              |
   | Interaction [Phytic Acid]: ~0.4-0.9% by weight in semolina. Chelates         |
   | multivalent cations (Ca2+, Mg2+, Zn2+) and binds drug molecules in the      |
   | stomach, forming insoluble complexes that cannot cross intestinal wall.      |
   | Levothyroxine: drug trapped in complex. Ciprofloxacin: direct chelation via  |
   | phosphate group binding. Confidence: HIGH for both.                          |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Ferulic Acid]: Most abundant phenolic acid in durum wheat.      |
   | Modulates P-glycoprotein (P-gp) efflux pump, affecting bioavailability of   |
   | P-gp substrates such as Digoxin. Confidence: MODERATE.                      |
   |                                                                               |
   | *** CORRECTION: Solanine (nightshades only) and Glucosinolates              |
   | (Brassicaceae only) are ABSENT from durum wheat. If a couscous dish          |
   | contains turnips, the glucosinolate interaction belongs on the Turnip        |
   | node, not the Couscous node. This distinction is critical for GCN accuracy. *|
   |                                                                               |
   | GCN Actions:                                                                  |
   |   - Phytic Acid (food_id=767 in unified-DFI): add edges to                  |
   |     Levothyroxine and Ciprofloxacin.                                         |
   |   - Ferulic Acid: add new food node (SMILES above), add edge to Digoxin.    |
   | Source: Journal of Food Science and Technology — PMCID: PMC4325062           |
   | RED SAUCE VARIANT: When Couscous is served with Marqa Hamra (tomato stew +   |
   | cinnamon stick), also add: Lycopene[✓]→Warfarin, K+(tomato)→ACE Inhibitors,    |
   | Cinnamaldehyde[✓]→Warfarin. See Chakhchoukha (03) for full SMILES detail.       |
   |                                                                               |

02 | Zfiti / Zviti             | Allicin [✓]                  | ACE Inhibitors (Lisinopril /
   | (Traditional Spicy Mash)  | SMILES: C=CCSS(=O)C=C       | Enalapril)
   |                           |                              | Warfarin / Antiplatelets
   |                           |------------------------------|
   |                           | Capsaicin [✓]                | Theophylline (Asthma)
   |                           | SMILES: CC(C)/C=C/CCCCC     |
   |                           | (=O)NCC1=CC(=C(C=C1)O)OC   |
   |                           |------------------------------|
   |                           | Piperine                     | Phenytoin (Epilepsy)
   |                           | SMILES: C1CCN(CC1)C(=O)     | Propranolol (Heart)
   |                           | /C=C/C=C/C2=CC3=C(C=C2)    |
   |                           | OCO3                         |
   |                           |------------------------------|
   |                           | Phytic Acid (IP6) [✓]        | Levothyroxine (Thyroid)
   |                           | (from Kesra — unfermented)  | Ciprofloxacin (Quinolones)
   |                           |                              |
   | Interaction [Allicin]: Natural ACE-inhibitory organosulfur compound. Crushing |
   | raw garlic in a Mahras releases Alliinase, converting Alliin → Allicin.      |
   | Additive vasodilation with ACE inhibitors → symptomatic hypotension. Also    |
   | inhibits platelet aggregation → additive effect with Warfarin/Antiplatelets. |
   | Confidence: HIGH for both.                                                    |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Capsaicin]: CYP1A2 inhibitor. Theophylline has a narrow         |
   | therapeutic index and is primarily metabolized by CYP1A2; even slight        |
   | inhibition raises plasma levels → risk of toxicity. Confidence: MEDIUM-HIGH. |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Piperine]: Inhibits P-glycoprotein (P-gp) and CYP3A4. Acts as  |
   | a bioavailability enhancer — elevates plasma levels of P-gp/CYP3A4           |
   | substrates (Phenytoin, Propranolol) to potentially dangerous levels.         |
   | Confidence: HIGH.                                                             |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Phytic Acid]: Kesra is unfermented bread — phytate levels are   |
   | significantly higher than leavened bread (fermentation/yeast degrades        |
   | phytates). Chelates Levothyroxine and Ciprofloxacin. Confidence: HIGH.       |
   |                                                                               |
   | GCN Actions:                                                                  |
   |   - Allicin [✓]: add edges to Lisinopril, Enalapril, Warfarin.              |
   |   - Capsaicin [✓]: add edge to Theophylline.                                |
   |   - Piperine: add new food node (SMILES above), add edges to Phenytoin,     |
   |     Propranolol.                                                              |
   |   - Phytic Acid (food_id=767): add edges to Levothyroxine, Ciprofloxacin.   |
   | Source: Journal of Ethnopharmacology — PMCID: PMC3210006                     |
   | RED SAUCE VARIANT: Zfiti is often served with a tomato marinade sauce; when  |
   | so, add: Lycopene[✓]→Warfarin, Potassium(tomato)→ACE Inhibitors,              |
   | Cinnamaldehyde[✓]→Warfarin. See Chakhchoukha (03) for full detail.            |
   |                                                                               |

03 | Chakhchoukha              | Amylopectin (Rougag dough)   | Antidiabetics (Insulin /
   | (Shredded Flatbread Stew) | SMILES: C(C1C(C(C(C(O1)   | Metformin)
   |                           | OCC2C(C(C(C(O2)O)O)O)O)   |
   |                           | O)O)O)O                     |
   |                           |------------------------------|
   |                           | Lycopene [✓]                 | Warfarin (Blood thinner)
   |                           | (full SMILES see below)      |
   |                           |------------------------------|
   |                           | Potassium (tomato stew)      | ACE Inhibitors (Lisinopril /
   |                           |                              | Enalapril)
   |                           |------------------------------|
   |                           | Cinnamaldehyde [✓]           | Warfarin (Blood thinner)
   |                           | SMILES: C1=CC=C(C=C1)       |
   |                           | C=CC=O                       |
   |                           |------------------------------|
   |                           | Capsaicin [✓]                | Theophylline (Asthma)
   |                           | (Ras harr — green chili)     |
   |                           |------------------------------|
   |                           | Purines / Tyramine           | Allopurinol (Gout)
   |                           | (Chicken + Chickpeas)        | MAOIs
   |                           |                              |
   | Lycopene SMILES: CC(=CCCC(=CCCC(=CCCC(=CCCC(=C)C=CC(=C)C=CC(=C)C=CC(=C)     |
   | C=CC=C(C)C=CC=C(C)C=CC=C(C)C=CC=C(C)CCC=C(C)C)C)C)C)C                       |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Amylopectin]: Fine semolina + flour dough rested 5h without     |
   | fermentation — starch stays compact. Thin translucent Rougag sheets expose  |
   | starch fully → rapid hydrolysis → fast postprandial glucose spike,          |
   | physically antagonizing Insulin/Metformin. Confidence: HIGH.                 |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Lycopene]: Oil-sautéed concentrated tomato sauce (4 tomatoes +  |
   | paste + oil) maximizes lycopene bioavailability. Anti-platelet properties    |
   | are additive with Warfarin. Confidence: MEDIUM-HIGH.                         |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Potassium]: Concentrated tomato stew is rich in Potassium. ACE  |
   | inhibitors reduce urinary K+ excretion → additive Hyperkalemia risk.         |
   | Confidence: HIGH.                                                             |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Cinnamaldehyde]: Simmering Oud Qerfa (cinnamon stick) releases  |
   | Cinnamaldehyde and Coumarin (if Cassia) → potentiates Warfarin. HIGH.        |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Capsaicin]: CYP1A2 inhibition from Ras harr chili → raises     |
   | Theophylline plasma levels. Confidence: MEDIUM-HIGH.                         |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Purines/Tyramine]: Long-simmered chicken + chickpeas release    |
   | purines (uric acid load, countering Allopurinol). If reheated, tyramine      |
   | accumulates → hypertensive crisis with MAOIs. Confidence: MEDIUM.           |
   |                                                                               |
   | GCN Actions:                                                                  |
   |   - Amylopectin: add new food node (SMILES above), add edges to Insulin,    |
   |     Metformin.                                                                |
   |   - Lycopene [✓]: add edge to Warfarin.                                     |
   |   - Potassium: add new node, add edges to Lisinopril, Enalapril.             |
   |   - Cinnamaldehyde [✓]: add/confirm edge to Warfarin.                       |
   |   - Capsaicin [✓]: confirm edge to Theophylline.                            |
   |   - Purines [✓]: confirm edge to Allopurinol.                              |
   | Source: Journal of Food Composition and Analysis — PMCID: PMC4325062         |
   |                                                                               |

04 | Guedid / Kadid            | Tyramine [✓]                 | MAOIs (Phenelzine /
   | (Dried Salted Meat)       | SMILES: NCCc1ccc(O)cc1      | Selegiline)
   |                           |------------------------------|
   |                           | Sodium (Na+) ~50 g/kg        | Lithium (Psychiatric)
   |                           |                              | ACE Inhibitors (Lisinopril)
   |                           |------------------------------|
   |                           | Allicin [✓]                  | Warfarin (Blood thinner)
   |                           | Diallyl Trisulfide           | HIV Protease Inhibitors
   |                           | SMILES: C=CCSS(=O)C=C       | (Saquinavir)
   |                           |------------------------------|
   |                           | AGEs (Advanced Glycation     | Antidiabetic Medications
   |                           | End-products)                | (long-term)
   |                           |                              |
   | Interaction [Tyramine]: Long drying process (up to 1 year) allows bacteria   |
   | to convert the amino acid Tyrosine → Tyramine. MAOIs block the MAO enzyme   |
   | that normally breaks down tyramine → massive norepinephrine release →         |
   | Hypertensive Crisis (life-threatening BP spike). Confidence: HIGH.           |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Sodium ~50 g/kg]: Electrolyte “bomb” from heavy salt cure.      |
   | Lithium: Na+ and Li+ compete for renal reabsorption; sudden high salt load   |
   | flushes Lithium out, dropping therapeutic levels below effective range.       |
   | ACE Inhibitors: high dietary sodium directly antagonizes blood-pressure-       |
   | lowering effect of Lisinopril etc. Confidence: HIGH for both.                |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Allicin / Diallyl Trisulfide]: Garlic used in brine/rub for     |
   | preservation. Organosulfur compounds inhibit platelet aggregation (additive  |
   | bleeding risk with Warfarin) and induce CYP3A4 (reduces plasma levels of    |
   | HIV protease inhibitors like Saquinavir). Confidence: MEDIUM-HIGH.           |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [AGEs]: Long air-exposure + trace sugars in rub → Maillard-      |
   | derived AGEs accumulate. Chronic intake raises inflammatory markers and has  |
   | pharmacodynamic conflict with antidiabetic drugs. Confidence: MEDIUM.        |
   |                                                                               |
   | NOTE: The “age” of the protein (curing duration) is as important as the      |
   | species. Fresh lamb ≠ Guedid at the GCN node level.                          |
   |                                                                               |
   | GCN Actions:                                                                  |
   |   - Tyramine [✓]: add/confirm edges to Phenelzine, Selegiline.              |
   |   - Sodium: add new node, add edges to Lithium, Lisinopril.                 |
   |   - Allicin [✓]: add edges to Warfarin, Saquinavir.                         |
   |   - AGEs: add new node, add edge to Metformin (antidiabetic class).          |
   | Source: Journal of Food Protection — DOI: 10.4315/0362-028X-68.7.1377       |
   |                                                                               |

05 | Osban / Osbane            | Stearic Acid / Triglycerides | Cyclosporine (Immunosupp.)
   | (Aromatic Offal Sausage)  | (Cha7ma — suet filling)      | Orlistat (Weight loss)
   |                           | SMILES: CCCCCCCCCCCCCCCC    |
   |                           | CCCC(=O)O                   |
   |                           |------------------------------|
   |                           | Apigenin [✓]                 | Benzodiazepines (Diazepam /
   |                           | (Parsley + Coriander/Debcha) | Alprazolam)
   |                           |------------------------------|
   |                           | Allicin [✓]                  | Warfarin / Antiplatelets
   |                           | Quercetin [✓]                | CYP2C9 substrates
   |                           | (Garlic + Onion — crushed,  |
   |                           | stuffed)                    |
   |                           |------------------------------|
   |                           | Purines [✓]                  | Allopurinol (Gout)
   |                           | (Intestines / Tripe)         |
   |                           |------------------------------|
   |                           | Piperine                     | Phenytoin (Epilepsy)
   |                           | (Spice blend)                | CYP3A4 / P-gp substrates
   |                           |                              |
   | Interaction [Saturated Fats/Triglycerides]: Cha7ma (suet) = pure animal fat.  |
   | Lipophilic drug absorption is dramatically altered by high-fat meals.         |
   | Cyclosporine: fat increases absorption → toxic blood levels possible. Orlistat:|
   | fat-blocking mechanism directly conflicts → severe GI side effects. HIGH.    |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Apigenin]: Parsley and fresh coriander (Debcha) are richest      |
   | dietary sources of Apigenin. Binds GABA-A receptors (same site as            |
   | benzodiazepines) → synergistic sedation → patient far more drowsy than       |
   | intended therapeutic dose. Confidence: MEDIUM-HIGH.                          |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Allicin + Quercetin]: Even a single crushed garlic clove and     |
   | onion in the stuffing release sulfur compounds. Allicin inhibits platelet     |
   | aggregation (additive bleeding risk with Warfarin). Quercetin inhibits        |
   | CYP2C9, the primary enzyme that clears Warfarin → elevated Warfarin levels.  |
   | Confidence: HIGH.                                                             |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Purines]: The sausage bag (intestines) and tripe filling are     |
   | high-purine tissues. Direct metabolic antagonism: raises uric acid, countering|
   | Allopurinol and other uricosuric drugs. Confidence: HIGH.                    |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Piperine]: Spice blend inclusion. CYP3A4 and P-gp inhibition    |
   | raises plasma levels of Phenytoin and other substrates. Confidence: HIGH.    |
   |                                                                               |
   | GCN Actions:                                                                  |
   |   - Stearic Acid/Triglycerides: add new node (SMILES above), add edges to   |
   |     Cyclosporine, Orlistat.                                                   |
   |   - Apigenin [✓]: add/confirm edge to Diazepam, Alprazolam.                 |
   |   - Allicin [✓]: confirm edge to Warfarin.                                  |
   |   - Quercetin [✓]: add edge to Warfarin (via CYP2C9 mechanism).             |
   |   - Purines [✓]: confirm edge to Allopurinol.                              |
   |   - Piperine: confirm new node (see Zviti 02), add edge to Phenytoin.        |
   | Source: Journal of Ethnopharmacology — DOI: 10.1016/j.jep.2004.10.019        |
   |                                                                               |

06 | Lahm Lhalou               | Cinnamaldehyde [✓]           | Warfarin (Blood thinner)
   | (Tajine el Hlou /         | SMILES: C1=CC=C(C=C1)       |
   | Sweet Tajine)             | C=CC=O                       |
   |                           |------------------------------|
   |                           | Coumarin [✓]                 | Warfarin (INR potentiation)
   |                           | SMILES: C1=CC=C2C(=C1)      |
   |                           | C=CC(=O)O2                  |
   |                           |------------------------------|
   |                           | Sucrose / Fructose           | Insulin / Metformin /
   |                           | (Sugar syrup + dried prunes, | SGLT2 Inhibitors
   |                           | apricots, raisins)           | (Empagliflozin)
   |                           | SMILES: C(C1C(C(C(C(O1)OC2  |
   |                           | (C(C(C(O2)CO)O)O)CO)O)O)O)O |
   |                           |------------------------------|
   |                           | Amygdalin [✓]                | CYP2D6 substrates
   |                           | (Almonds + fruit pits —      | (Codeine, Tamoxifen)
   |                           | Rosaceae family)             |
   |                           |------------------------------|
   |                           | Butyric Acid (Smen)          | Statins (Simvastatin /
   |                           | (Fermented clarified butter) | Atorvastatin)
   |                           | SMILES: CCCC(=O)O            |
   |                           |                              |
   | Interaction [Cinnamaldehyde + Coumarin]: Cassia cinnamon (most common type)  |
   | is rich in Coumarin — the structural precursor to Warfarin. Heavy cinnamon    |
   | syrup in this dish potentiates Warfarin's effect, significantly raising INR  |
   | → risk of spontaneous bleeding. Confidence: HIGH.                            |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Sucrose/Fructose]: Concentrated syrup of sugar + dried fruit =  |
   | massive glucose loading event. Pharmacodynamic antagonism with all anti-     |
   | diabetic drugs: can overwhelm insulin dose → acute hyperglycemia. HIGH.      |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Amygdalin]: Prunes, apricots, almonds (Rosaceae) contain       |
   | cyanogenic glycosides. At festive serving sizes, metabolites may interfere   |
   | with CYP2D6 hepatic pathways. Confidence: LOW-MEDIUM.                        |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Butyric Acid/Smen]: Fermented clarified butter = high-fat       |
   | vehicle. Increases absorption of lipophilic drugs (Statins). Combined with   |
   | sugar spike affecting liver lipid processing → alters Statin PK. MED.       |
   |                                                                               |
   | GCN Actions:                                                                  |
   |   - Cinnamaldehyde [✓]: confirm edge to Warfarin.                           |
   |   - Coumarin [✓]: confirm edge to Warfarin.                                 |
   |   - Sucrose: add new node (SMILES above), add edges to Insulin, Metformin.  |
   |   - Amygdalin [✓]: confirm edge to CYP2D6 substrates.                      |
   |   - Butyric Acid (Smen): add new node (SMILES above), add edge to           |
   |     Simvastatin.                                                              |
   | Source: German BfR Opinion No. 044/2006; PMID: 17594919                     |
   |                                                                               |

07 | Chorba Frik / Jari               | Phytic Acid (IP6) [✓]        | Fluoroquinolones
   | (Green Smoked Wheat Soup) | + Magnesium                  | (Ciprofloxacin / Ofloxacin)
   |                           | (Frik = green-stage wheat;   | Tetracyclines (Doxycycline)
   |                           | higher IP6 + Mg than mature  | Iron Supplements
   |                           | grain — primary chelators)    | (Gut chelation → complex
   |                           | SMILES (Phytic Acid):        | too large to absorb →
   |                           | OP(=O)(O)OC1C(OP(=O)(O)O)   | treatment failure)
   |                           | C(OP(=O)(O)O)C(OP(=O)(O)O)  |
   |                           | C(OP(=O)(O)O)C1OP(=O)(O)O   |
   |                           |------------------------------|
   |                           | Smoked Phenolics / PAHs      | CYP1A2 Substrates:
   |                           | (Parching process — Frik     | Theophylline, Caffeine,
   |                           | prepared by burning wheat    | Duloxetine, Clozapine
   |                           | ears; introduces PAHs and    | (CYP1A2 induction →
   |                           | char phenols)                | accelerated clearance
   |                           | SMILES (Benzo[a]pyrene rep.):| → sub-therapeutic levels)
   |                           | C1=CC=C2C3=C4C(=CC=C3)C5=CC |
   |                           | =CC=C5C4=CC2=C1              |
   |                           |------------------------------|
   |                           | Soluble Fiber / Polysacc.    | Digoxin (Heart)
   |                           | (Pumpkin + Potato + Carrot   | Metformin
   |                           | blended into broth —         | All oral medications
   |                           | thickened mash matrix)       | (↑ gastric viscosity →
   |                           | SMILES (Pectin rep.): OC1OC  | ↓ absorption rate →
   |                           | (C(=O)O)CC(O)C1O             | sub-therapeutic Digoxin)
   |                           |------------------------------|
   |                           | Rosmarinic Acid & Menthol [✓]| Iron Supplements
   |                           | (Fresh Cilantro + Mint added | (Non-heme iron absorption
   |                           | at end — volatile oils       | inhibited 50–70% by
   |                           | retained)                    | herb polyphenols)
   |                           | SMILES (Rosmarinic Acid):    |
   |                           | OC(=O)C(Cc1ccc(O)c(O)c1)OC  |
   |                           | (=O)/C=C/c1ccc(O)c(O)c1     |
   |                           |                              |
   | Interaction [Phytic Acid + Mg / Frik]: Frik is harvested at the green stage    |
   | before full grain maturation, retaining a higher concentration of Phytic Acid  |
   | (IP6) and Magnesium than mature wheat. Both are powerful chelators: they bind  |
   | Fluoroquinolones (Ciprofloxacin) and Tetracyclines in the GI tract → insoluble|
   | metal-antibiotic complexes → excreted without absorption → treatment failure. |
   | Critical clinical scenario: a patient taking Cipro for a UTI eats Chorba Frik / Jari |
   | during Ramadan Iftar with their evening dose → the entire dose is inactivated. |
   | Confidence: HIGH.                                                              |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Smoked Phenolics / PAHs]: Traditional Frik preparation requires  |
   | burning the wheat ears (parching). This introduces PAHs and char-derived       |
   | phenols into the grain. Smoked compounds are potent CYP1A2 INDUCERS (same     |
   | pathway as cigarette smoke). Frequent consumption (nightly during Ramadan)    |
   | accelerates metabolism of CYP1A2 substrates → Theophylline/Duloxetine fail    |
   | to reach therapeutic plasma levels. Confidence: MEDIUM-HIGH.                 |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Blended Vegetable Mash / Fiber]: Pumpkin, potato, and carrots    |
   | are blended directly into the broth (not strained), creating a viscous fiber- |
   | rich matrix. Increases stomach content viscosity → slows diffusion of Digoxin |
   | and Metformin into intestinal wall → sub-therapeutic peaks. Confidence: MEDIUM.|
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Rosmarinic Acid + Menthol]: Fresh cilantro and mint added at end |
   | to preserve volatile oils. Herb polyphenols (Rosmarinic Acid) inhibit non-heme|
   | iron absorption by 50–70% when consumed simultaneously. Also synergises with  |
   | Phytic Acid iron-chelation (double-block on iron in the same bowl).           |
   | Confidence: HIGH.                                                              |
   |                                                                               |
   | NOTE: Chorba Frik / Jari is the most consumed Ramadan soup in Algeria (near-daily).  |
   | The PAH/CYP1A2 induction effect is clinically meaningful ONLY with prolonged  |
   | repeated exposure — Ramadan provides exactly that window (30 consecutive      |
   | evenings). Combine with the antibiotic-chelation risk at Iftar → Chorba Frik / Jari  |
   | is the highest Ramadan-context risk dish in the entire dataset.               |
   |                                                                               |
   | GCN Actions:                                                                  |
   |   - Phytic Acid [✓]: shared node (01/02/19/21/25); confirm Ciprofloxacin +  |
   |     Iron edges; add new edge: Tetracyclines.                                 |
   |   - Magnesium: shared node; co-chelation annotation with Phytic Acid →       |
   |     Fluoroquinolones edge.                                                   |
   |   - Smoked Phenolics / PAHs: new node; CYP1A2-INDUCER edge (distinct from   |
   |     CYP1A2 INHIBITOR edges elsewhere); annotate Ramadan-repeated-exposure.  |
   |   - Soluble Fiber / Pectin: shared node (25 Beta-Glucan parallel); confirm  |
   |     Digoxin + Metformin delayed-absorption edges.                            |
   |   - Rosmarinic Acid: new node; INHIBITORY edge → Iron Supplements;          |
   |     annotate double-block with Phytic Acid in same dish.                    |
   | Source: Am J Clin Nutr DOI: 10.1093/ajcn/39.4.645 (fiber/Digoxin);          |
   |         Lomaestro BM, Ann Pharmacother 1995 (chelation/Cipro);              |
   |         Conney AH, Cancer Res 1982 (PAH/CYP1A2 induction)                 |
   |                                                                               |

08 | Atay                     | Tannins (Polyphenols) [✓]   | Iron Supplements
   | (Green Tea — Saharan/      | (Prolonged boiling 30-60     | Calcium Supplements
   |  Tuareg Triple Infusion;  | mins maximises extraction;   | Zinc / Magnesium
   |  Kashkasha method)        | 1st-pot concentration 3-5×  | (Strong chelation:
   |                           | higher than standard steep;  | tannins form insoluble
   |                           | tannin content rivals black  | metal-tannate complexes
   |                           | tea; catechins + gallic acid | in gut → iron absorption
   |                           | contribute to chelation      | reduced 60-70%;
   |                           | load)                        | critical warning for
   |                           | SMILES (Epigallocatechin     | anaemia patients;
   |                           | gallate, EGCG):              | same mechanism blocks
   |                           | O=C(O[C@@H]1Cc2c(O)cc(O)    | Ca²⁺ and Zn²⁺ supplementation)
   |                           | cc2O[C@@H]1c1cc(O)c(O)c(O)  |
   |                           | c1)c1cc(O)c(O)c(O)c1         |
   |                           |------------------------------|
   |                           | Caffeine (Theine) [✓]        | Ciprofloxacin
   |                           | (Cooked until “qawi” —       | Clozapine
   |                           | strong; caffeine             | Theophylline
   |                           | concentration 200-400mg/L    | Benzodiazepines
   |                           | vs. 40-80mg/L standard       | Beta-Blockers
   |                           | green tea; 1st pot is        | (CYP1A2 substrate:
   |                           | highest-caffeine glass in    | Cipro inhibits CYP1A2
   |                           | the dataset among            | → caffeine toxicity
   |                           | beverages)                   | (palpitations, insomnia);
   |                           | SMILES:                      | Clozapine levels ↑
   |                           | Cn1cnc2c1c(=O)n(C)c(=O)n2C  | due to same pathway;
   |                           |                              | Caffeine antagonises
   |                           |                              | benzodiazepine/BB
   |                           |                              | sedative effects)
   |                           |------------------------------|
   |                           | Saponins (Froth node)        | Oral Capsules
   |                           | (Kashkasha pouring method    | Dissolution-sensitive
   |                           | — back-and-forth between     | drugs
   |                           | pots — generates signature   | (Surface tension
   |                           | froth; saponins reduce       | alteration: saponins
   |                           | surface tension of gastric   | change gastric surface
   |                           | contents; unique to Saharan  | tension → may alter
   |                           | preparation technique in     | dissolution rate of
   |                           | this dataset)                | oral capsules; effect
   |                           |                              | mild but mechanistically
   |                           |                              | present; unique node
   |                           |                              | in dataset)
   |                           |------------------------------|
   |                           | Sucrose (High-load)          | Insulin
   |                           | (Traditional recipe: 3-5     | Metformin
   |                           | tbsp sugar per small-glass   | SGLT2 Inhibitors
   |                           | service; Sahrawi glasses     | (Glycaemic load:
   |                           | are small (50-80ml) but      | high-sugar concentration
   |                           | multiple rounds consumed;    | in small rapid glasses
   |                           | cumulative sucrose load       | → rapid spikes if taken
   |                           | across 3 rounds is HIGH)     | between meals;
   |                           | SMILES (Sucrose):            | 3rd-pot glass = mostly
   |                           | C([C@@H]1[C@H]([C@@H]([C@H](| hydration + sugar,
   |                           | [C@H](O1)O[C@]2([C@H]([C@@H]| minimal bioactives)
   |                           | ([C@H](O2)CO)O)O)CO)O)O)O)O |
   |                           |                              |
   | Interaction [Tannin-Iron Chelation / Anaemia Risk]: Long boiling (30-60 min)   |
   | maximises polyphenol extraction from green tea leaves. Tannins and gallic-acid |
   | derivatives form insoluble complexes with Fe²⁺, Fe³⁺, Ca²⁺, and Zn²⁺ in the    |
   | intestinal lumen. Iron absorption is reduced 60-70% when tea is taken with or  |
   | within 1 hour of iron supplements or iron-rich meals. This is the strongest    |
   | tannin-chelation signal in the dataset (stronger than single-steep teas) due   |
   | to the prolonged extraction. GCN: STRONG INHIBITION edge, Tannin →            |
   | Iron_Absorption. Critical flag for thalassaemia/anaemia patients.              |
   | Confidence: HIGH.                                                              |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Concentrated Caffeine / CYP1A2 Competition]: The “qawi” (strong)  |
   | preparation yields caffeine levels 3-5× standard green tea. CYP1A2 is the     |
   | primary caffeine metabolising enzyme. Ciprofloxacin (and other fluoroquinolones)|
   | inhibit CYP1A2 → caffeine half-life doubles or triples → caffeine toxicity     |
   | (tachycardia, tremor, insomnia). Clozapine shares CYP1A2: elevated plasma      |
   | levels risk. Theophylline (also CYP1A2) shows additive xanthine burden.        |
   | Conversely, caffeine competitively antagonises benzodiazepine and beta-blocker |
   | sedation/rate-control at the receptor level (pharmacodynamic antagonism).      |
   | GCN: METABOLIC_INTERFERENCE edge, CYP1A2 node. Confidence: HIGH.              |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Triple Infusion / Fractional Extraction Model]: The Sahrawi       |
   | tradition serves three sequential rounds from the same leaves:                 |
   |   1st pot: Highest caffeine + tannins (Bitter Stimulant) — maximum drug       |
   |             interaction risk; CYP1A2 competition + chelation both peak.       |
   |   2nd pot: Balanced flavonoids, moderate caffeine — intermediate risk.        |
   |   3rd pot: Low bioactives, high water + sugar (Hydrator, served to children) |
   |             — glycaemic load from sucrose persists but pharmacological nodes  |
   |             are attenuated. GCN annotation: edge weights decrease 1→2→3 pot. |
   | Confidence: HIGH (fractional extraction well-characterised for green tea).     |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Saponins / Kashkasha Froth — Surface Tension Node]: The           |
   | pouring technique (الخلط من ابريق لآخر) aerates the tea and generates saponin-rich |
   | froth. Saponins are surfactants that reduce gastric surface tension. For       |
   | dissolution-sensitive oral medications (certain capsules, hydrophobic tablets),|
   | altered surface tension can change the dissolution microenvironment. Effect is |
   | mild at beverage concentrations but represents a unique interaction node not   |
   | present in any other dish in this dataset. Confidence: MEDIUM.                 |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Sucrose / Glycaemic Load Across 3 Rounds]: Each small Sahrawi     |
   | glass (50-80ml) contains 3-5 tbsp sugar dissolved in a concentrated liquid.    |
   | Consuming all three rounds delivers significant sucrose load rapidly. For        |
   | patients on Insulin, Metformin, or SGLT2 inhibitors, inter-meal glycaemic       |
   | spikes from Atay consumption (common at Saharan tea ceremonies lasting 1-2 hrs)|
   | are clinically relevant. 3rd pot: minimal bioactives but sugar content remains.|
   | Confidence: HIGH.                                                              |
   |                                                                               |
   | GCN Cross-Note: Tannin node shared (Atay = highest extraction intensity in     |
   | dataset); Caffeine/CYP1A2 node unique to this dish (only caffeinated beverage);|
   | Saponin/froth node unique to dish 08; Sucrose shared (06/14/32/49).            |
   | RAMADAN FLAG: Atay is consumed at Sahour and after Iftar — if taken within 1hr |
   | of iron supplements, chelation block is near-complete.                         |
   |                                                                               |
   | Source: Samman S, Br J Nutr 2001 (Tannins/iron absorption inhibition 60-70%); |
   |         Harder S, Eur J Clin Pharmacol 1988 (Ciprofloxacin/CYP1A2/caffeine);  |
   |         Han Y, Br J Clin Pharmacol 2009 (Green tea/Clozapine/CYP1A2);         |
   |         Bhakta D, Proc Nutr Soc 2002 (Saponins gastric surface tension)       |
   |                                                                               |

   |                           | Amylopectin (Pasta, simmered | Insulin / Metformin

   | Interaction [Phytic Acid — Primary Chelator]: Legumes are the highest        |

   | significant IP6 remains. Phytic acid binds metallic ions (Fe²⁺, Ca²⁺, Mg²⁺,  |

   |                                                                               |

   |                                                                               |
   | NOTE: Doubara is the primary "Absorption Interference" node in the dataset.  |
   | The Phytic Acid chelation mechanism is unique — unlike CYP or P-gp effects   |

   | METABOLISM INHIBITION. Edge polarity annotation required.                    |

   | Tramadol is widely used, this is a clinically significant interaction. Poor   |
   | pain control reported even at standard doses. Confidence: HIGH.               |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Purines — Triple Load]: Beef/Lamb chunks + minced meat +          |
   | chickpeas = three concurrent purine sources. This is the highest multi-source |
   | purine load for any single dish in the dataset. Xanthine oxidase inhibitors   |
   | (Allopurinol / Febuxostat) can be overwhelmed → acute gout flare triggered    |
   | even in a patient who is otherwise stable on medication. Confidence: HIGH.    |
   |                                                                               |
   | NOTE: Harira is also consumed during Ramadan (Iftar), meaning patients who    |
   | have been fasting all day then take their daily medications at Iftar — the   |
   | Beta-glucan matrix from Mermez simultaneously delays all drug absorption at   |
   | exactly the moment the full daily dose is administered. Clinical significance  |
   | is amplified during Ramadan fasting cycles.                                   |
   |                                                                               |
   | GCN Actions:                                                                  |
   |   - Beta-Glucan: new node; DELAYED-ABSORPTION edge to all oral drug nodes;  |
   |     annotate Tmax-extension property; flag Ramadan-context amplification.   |
   |   - Apigenin [✓]: shared node; confirm Benzodiazepine + Paclitaxel edges;  |
   |     add NEW edges: Furosemide + ACE-I (phthalide co-effect).                |
   |   - Carvone [✓]: shared node; confirm CYP2D6 + Codeine/Tramadol edges.    |
   |   - Purines [✓]: shared node; annotate TRIPLE-SOURCE highest-load;         |

   | Interaction [Phytic Acid + Non-Heme Iron / Lentils]: Lentils are among the     |
   | highest plant sources of both iron and phytic acid simultaneously. Phytic Acid |
   | chelates the non-heme iron itself (reducing the dish’s own iron bioavailability)|
   | and also chelates co-administered drugs (Ciprofloxacin, Levothyroxine) in the  |
   | gut. Free Fe²⁺/Fe³⁺ released into the broth add a second direct chelation     |
   | pathway for Levothyroxine. A thyroid patient taking their dose with this soup  |
   | faces chelation from BOTH Phytic Acid and free iron concurrently — double-     |
   | mechanism absorption block. Confidence: HIGH.                                 |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Late-Added Allicin]: Recipe-critical: garlic is added only in the |
   | final 3 minutes. At this temperature and duration, alliinase is not fully      |
   | denatured, resulting in a significantly higher concentration of intact Allicin  |
   | compared to dishes where garlic is fried or sauteed at the start (e.g. dishes  |
   | 10, 17, 24). Anti-platelet and vasodilatory effects are at maximum potency.    |
   | GCN: this Allicin node should carry a STRONGER synergistic edge weight to      |
   | Warfarin/anticoagulants than long-cooked-garlic dishes. Confidence: HIGH.     |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [K⁺ / Quad Source]: Potato + Carrot + 3 Turnips + Lentils is the  |

   | Patients on Lisinopril or Spironolactone are at significant hyperkalaemia risk  |

   | Confidence: HIGH.                                                              |
   |                                                                               |

   | volatile Cuminaldehyde content without thermal degradation. This gives maximum  |

   | or hypoglycaemia. Same late-addition technique as late garlic — both are       |
   | deliberately preserved by the cook, making this pharmacologically intentional. |
   | Confidence: HIGH.                                                              |
   |                                                                               |
   | NOTE: Adas has two “late-addition” nodes (Allicin + Cuminaldehyde)     |
   | where the cooking technique deliberately maximises bioactive potency. This is   |

   | Interaction [Saturated Fats / Deep-Frying — Lipid Hub]: Bourak is deep-fried   |
   | until golden. The large lipid bolus triggers substantial bile acid secretion    |
   | → increased solubility of all fat-soluble medications in the GI lumen. Effect  |
   | can double peak plasma concentration (Cmax) of Atorvastatin, Cyclosporine,    |
   | and Isotretinoin. HIV ARVs (many are lipophilic) are also affected. Annotated  |
   | as “Lipid Hub” due to the non-specific, dish-wide amplifier effect.            |
   | Confidence: HIGH.                                                              |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Amylopectin / Dioul + Potato]: Refined semolina (Dioul) and       |

   | mass ratio of fried dough accelerates starch hydrolysis further. Combined      |

   | Insulin (dose taken before meal may not match the actual glucose surge) or     |

   | Interaction [Honey-Lipid Synergy / Fast-Release Sugar Hub]: Zlabia is unique    |
   | in this dataset for combining BOTH maximum-velocity gelatinised starch AND       |
   | 250g honey saturation. The deep-frying thermally opens pore channels in the     |
   | semolina shell; immediate honey submersion floods the core. The result is a     |
   | simultaneous delivery of gelatinised starch glucose + honey fructose/glucose.  |
   | For patients on Metformin: WORST-CASE SCENARIO node — the combined sugar and    |
   | starch load may cause dumping syndrome or severe glucose spike the medication   |
   | cannot counteract. GCN: EXTREME VELOCITY on Glycaemic_Index node, highest       |
   | sugar-starch combined score in whole dataset. Confidence: HIGH.                |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Gelatinised Starch / Alpha-Amylase Pre-Access]: The unique        |
   | preparation step of boiling water-oil-sugar then beating in semolina (قلب      |
   | المزيج ليصبح كتلة واحدة) gelatinises starch granules before any frying occurs.  |
   | Gelatinised starch exposes amylopectin chains fully to alpha-amylase on        |
   | ingestion, converting to glucose at maximum enzymatic rate. This exceeds even  |
   | the porous-Baghrir glycaemic velocity because there is NO intact granule        |
   | structure remaining to slow digestion. GCN edge weight: EXTREME (> Baghrir,   |
   | > Mhadjeb, > any flatbread). Confidence: HIGH.                                |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [250g Honey / Osmotic Stress + Diuretic Risk]: The osmotic         |
   | pressure of concentrated honey (water activity ~0.6) draws fluid into the     |
   | intestinal lumen. In patients taking loop diuretics (Furosemide, Torsemide)   |
   | for hypertension or heart failure, this GI osmotic pull compounds the drug’s  |
   | diuretic-induced fluid loss. Risk: dehydration, hyponatraemia, hypokalaemia.  |
   | SGLT2 inhibitors also cause osmotic glucosuria — the honey osmotic load is     |
   | additive. GCN: NEGATIVE_INTERACTION edge from High_Sucrose_Node to            |
   | Osmotic_Balance for renal/diuretic medications. Confidence: HIGH.             |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Deep-Fry Lipid + Lecithin / Fat-Soluble Drug Cmax]: The lipid    |
   | bolus from deep-frying triggers bile secretion for fat-soluble drug Cmax      |
   | elevation (same mechanism as dishes 28/43). Egg lecithin simultaneously        |
   | enlarges the micellar surface available for lipid absorption, further enhancing|
   | transport of fat-soluble co-administered medications. Combined fat + emulsifier |
   | = one of the strongest lipid-bioavailability signals in the dataset.           |
   | Confidence: HIGH.                                                              |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Lemon Zest / Minor CYP3A4]: Limonene from lemon zest is a        |
   | documented mild CYP3A4 inducer at pharmacological doses. At typical garnish    |
   | quantity, the effect is minor on Cyclosporine/Tacrolimus metabolism. Digestive |
   | enzyme stimulation slightly accelerates gastric processing but does not         |
   | materially alter drug Tmax at garnish-level consumption. Confidence: LOW-MED.  |
   |                                                                               |
   | GCN POSITION: Zlabia = HIGHEST combined sugar+starch glycaemic score in        |
   | dataset. Shares Gelatinised_Starch node (unique — only dish with pre-gelatinised|
   | dough); Honey_Fructose node shared with Baghrir (optional) and Bradj;          |
   | Lecithin node shared with dishes 05/27/43; DeepFry_Lipid shared with 28/43.   |
   | RAMADAN FLAG: Zlabia is the quintessential Iftar sweet — consumed fasted;      |
   | post-fast insulin sensitivity spike + this dish = maximum hyperglycaemic risk. |

   | Interaction [Lamination Effect / High-Latency Glycaemic Profile]:               |
   | Baklawa’s defining GCN characteristic is HIGH LATENCY glucose rather than        |
   | extreme velocity. The fat barrier between every flour layer waterproofs each     |
   | sheet from digestive enzyme water access. Alpha-amylase requires aqueous         |
   | contact with starch — the lipid barrier forces enzymes to work layer-by-layer    |
   | from cut edges inward. Combined with the 1.5kg honey soak (which is itself       |
   | already digested quickly), the net effect is: initial rapid honey-derived        |
   | glucose, followed by a sustained slow-release starch glucose tail over 3-5h.    |
   | This biphasic profile is unique in the dataset. GCN differentiates Baklawa from |
   | Zlabia (instant extreme spike) and Baghrir (fast single peak). For patients on   |
   | long-acting Insulin (Glargine, Detemir) or Metformin, the prolonged glucose     |
   | tail is more relevant than the initial peak. Confidence: HIGH.                  |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Smen/Butter 200-400g / Maximum Lipophilic Drug Cmax]:              |
   | The inter-layer saturated fat load across a full Baklawa tray is the highest     |
   | total butter volume in the dataset. On a per-serving basis (2-4 pieces), the    |
   | fat bolus is comparable to Mbessas and exceeds single-egg dishes. Bile acid      |
   | secretion is maximised, creating peak micellar solubilisation for any           |
   | co-ingested fat-soluble medication. Amiodarone (a highly lipophilic drug with   |
   | a volume of distribution >60 L/kg) is particularly susceptible: its Cmax can   |
   | increase substantially with a high-fat meal like Baklawa. For patients on       |
   | Cyclosporine post-transplant consuming Baklawa at Eid: HIGH bioavailability     |
   | amplification risk. GCN: HIGHEST-BIOAVAIL edge on Lipid_Vector node.            |
   | Confidence: HIGH.                                                               |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Nut Node / VARIANT-CONDITIONAL — Walnut vs Almond vs Kawakaw]:     |
   | The nut filling is the primary variant driver in Baklawa’s GCN node.             |
   | Walnut: ALA omega-3 → mild TXA₂ inhibition (antiplatelet, additive Aspirin).   |
   | Almond: L-Arginine (~2.4g/100g) + Amygdalin; Amygdalin releases trace HCN      |
   | during digestion, micro-dose cytochrome interaction relevant with Amiodarone    |
   | (Amiodarone itself inhibits multiple CYP enzymes including CYP2D6/CYP3A4).      |
   | Peanut (Kawakaw): HIGHEST Arginine (~3.1g/100g) + Resveratrol; Arginine → eNOS |
   | → NO → vasodilation; for patients on Sildenafil or high-dose organic nitrates,  |
   | peanut-Baklawa has the strongest NO-synergy vascular drop risk in the dataset   |
   | (shared node with Shbah el-Safra dish 50 but at even higher Arginine density). |
   | GCN: VARIANT-CONDITIONAL edges on Arginine_NO and Antiplatelet nodes.           |
   | Confidence: HIGH (Arginine/NO mechanism); MEDIUM (Amygdalin/Amiodarone trace). |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [1.5kg Honey / Osmotic Pressure — Lithium & Digoxin Risk]:          |
   | 1.5kg honey in a single baking batch creates the highest honey osmotic load in  |
   | the dataset (6× Zlabia’s 250g). Per 3-4 piece serving, the honey component     |
   | delivers a concentrated fructose/glucose/sucrose solution. This creates a high  |
   | osmotic gradient in the intestinal lumen, drawing water from systemic           |
   | circulation. For patients on Lithium (narrow therapeutic index; renal clearance |
   | sensitive to fluid balance) this osmotic dehydration can raise serum Lithium    |
   | to toxic levels. For patients on Digoxin (NTI; hypokalaemia from dehydration    |
   | increases toxicity risk), osmotic water flux is doubly dangerous. SGLT2         |
   | inhibitors add a third osmotic pathway (glucosuria). GCN: Osmotic_Pressure tag  |
   | on Renal_Filtration + Volume_of_Distribution flag for NTI drugs. Confidence:   |
   | HIGH (Lithium/Digoxin fluid sensitivity well established).                      |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Rose + Blossom Water / Dual-Stage Linalool Absorption]:            |
   | Rose water (Geraniol) and orange blossom water (Linalool) appear in BOTH the   |
   | dough and the finishing syrup. This creates two absorption events: (1) from     |
   | the baked dough during the chewing/digestion phase, (2) from the syrup soaking  |
   | the outer surface on first contact. The cumulative Linalool dose is higher than |
   | in dishes where flower water is a single garnish. GABA-A potentiation with      |
   | benzodiazepines is additive; note also that the mild vagal calming effect of    |
   | Linalool partially counteracts the sympathomimetic component of the sugar rush, |
   | which explains the traditional perception of Baklawa as “calming” despite high  |
   | sugar content. Confidence: MEDIUM (dual-stage dose plausible; vagal mechanism   |
   | indirect).                                                                      |
   |                                                                               |
   | GCN POSITION: Baklawa = HIGHEST HONEY volume in dataset (1.5kg; 6× Zlabia);     |
   | HIGHEST inter-layer butter load in pastry category; HIGH-LATENCY biphasic       |
   | glucose profile (unique: honey fast-phase + starch slow-phase = dual-peak);     |
   | VARIANT-CONDITIONAL nut node (Walnut/Almond/Kawakaw changes NO-synergy          |
   | intensity); Osmotic NTI-Drug flag (Lithium/Digoxin) shared with Zlabia but at  |
   | higher honey volume; Linalool+Geraniol shared with Bradj(49), Makroud(34),      |
   | Shbah(50) — Baklawa is 4th floral-water dish; Amygdalin node shared with        |
   | Lahm Lhalou(06). RAMADAN/Eid FLAG: primary celebration sweet;                  |
   | multiple servings over Eid period = cumulative Cmax amplification risk.         |

   | Interaction [Non-Cohesive Sandy Matrix / Moderate-Slow Glycaemic Node]:        |
   | The defining preparation instruction for Makroud is “La Taʿjen” (do NOT knead).|
   | This means gluten is never developed, and the Smen/Butter stay as a surface    |
   | coating on each semolina granule rather than integrating into a dough matrix.  |
   | The result is a “Non-Cohesive Matrix”: digestive juices can physically          |
   | penetrate the crumb (unlike dense Ftir), but the fat layer on each granule     |
   | chemically blocks alpha-amylase. GCN edge weight on Starch_Digestion:          |
   | MODERATE-SLOW — slower than Mhadjeb/Baghrir, faster than Mbessas. Position    |
   | in glycaemic spectrum: Mbessas(47) < Ftir(48) < Makroud(34) < Matlouh(46) <   |
   | Mhadjeb(44) < Baghrir(45) < Zlabia(32). Confidence: HIGH.                     |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Baking 180°C / Liver-Safe Thermal Signature]:                    |
   | Unlike deep-fried dishes (Brik/Zlabia/Bourak), baking at 180°C does not reach  |
   | the lipid smoke point. Aldehydes, peroxides, and acrylamide formation are      |
   | minimal. This means Makroud has a NEUTRAL/SAFE edge to Liver_Metabolism in     |
   | the GCN — in contrast to the CYP2E1-induction signal present in all fried      |
   | dishes. For patients on hepatotoxic long-term medications (paracetamol ≥ 2g/d, |
   | isoniazid, methotrexate), Makroud poses no additional oxidative liver burden. |
   | Confidence: HIGH. Unique status: only baked semolina sweet in dataset.         |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Ghers Date Paste / K⁺ + Mg²⁺ Mineral Flux]:                       |
   | The centre filling is pure ʿAjwa-style date paste. Dates provide one of the   |
   | richest combined K⁺+Mg²⁺ profiles of any whole food: ~480mg K + ~54mg Mg per  |
   | 100g. Mg²⁺ acts as a physiological calcium-channel antagonist at vascular      |
   | smooth muscle, supporting vasodilation. This additive hypotensive effect is    |
   | GCN-linked as Ghers → Magnesium_Levels → Vasodilation. For patients on Ca-    |
   | channel blockers (Amlodipine, Felodipine) or K-sparing diuretics, the mineral  |
   | density of the date centre is the primary Red Flag. Confidence: HIGH.          |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Cinnamon + Cloves / Double CYP2C9 + Glucose Modulation]:          |
   | Makroud shares the Cinnamaldehyde+Eugenol double-CYP2C9 node with Bradj (49). |
   | However, the lipid and osmotic load here is lower, so Warfarin INR risk is     |
   | present but at MEDIUM rather than HIGH severity. Cinnamaldehyde also exerts    |
   | an insulin-sensitising effect (NEGATIVE-WEIGHT on Glycaemic_Spike node),        |
   | partially counteracting the date sugar load. Combined anti-inflammatory effect  |
   | of cinnamon + cloves relevant for patients on NSAIDs long-term.                |
   | Confidence: MEDIUM-HIGH.                                                       |
   |                                                                               |
   | GCN POSITION: Makroud = BAKED-SEMOLINA node (unique in dataset — only baked,   |
   | non-fried semolina sweet); Non_Cohesive_Matrix is new GCN node type (no        |
   | kneading → lipid-surface-coated granule architecture); Ghers_Mineral_Hub       |
   | shared with Bradj (49) date content; Double-CYP2C9 shared with Bradj (49);    |
   | Linalool_GABA shared with Bradj (49) and Shbah el-Safra (50). Makroud is the  |

   | Interaction [Friable Shortcrust Matrix / Controlled Slow Glucose — Aryan]:      |
   | The no-knead instruction (تجمع العجينة بدون دلكها) prevents gluten           |
   | network formation, leaving each flour particle fat-coated and structurally       |
   | independent. The dough shatters easily under mechanical stress (chewing) but    |
   | the fat coating on each granule resists hydration by digestive enzymes in the   |
   | gut. This “Friable Matrix” is similar to Makroud’s Non-Cohesive Matrix (dish 34)  |
   | but with a higher Smen-to-flour ratio — making the chemical barrier stronger.    |
   | GCN edge: SLOW weight on Starch_Digestion. Position on glycaemic spectrum:      |
   | slower than Makroud(34), approaching Mbessas(47) territory. Confidence: HIGH.  |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Biphasic Sucrose Burst / Meskar Version]:                         |
   | Meskar’s defining feature is the external powdered sugar coat. Ultra-fine        |
   | powdered sucrose has the largest surface area of any sugar form and dissolves   |
   | within seconds of mouth contact, releasing glucose and fructose before any      |
   | digestion of the dough occurs. This creates a sharp, immediate sucrose peak     |
   | followed by the slow fat-matrix starch tail. The biphasic profile is GCN-       |
   | distinct from Baklawa’s honey-fast + starch-slow dual peak: Meskar’s initial    |
   | spike is faster (instant dissolution vs. honey absorption) but lower volume     |
   | (thin sugar coat vs. 1.5kg honey). For patients on rapid-acting Insulin         |
   | (Lispro, Aspart), the immediate sucrose burst may outpace the insulin dose if   |
   | timed sub-optimally. GCN: Sucrose_Dust node → RAPID-BURST edge (fastest         |
   | sucrose delivery in dataset). Confidence: HIGH.                                |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Smen / Bile Acid Maximiser — Both Versions]:                       |
   | The Kila of Smen dominates both versions. High saturated fat density per        |
   | serving maximises bile acid secretion and micellar solubilisation of fat-soluble|
   | medications (Statins, Cyclosporine, Vitamin D, Isotretinoin). This mechanism   |
   | is shared with Mbessas(47), Makroud(34), Bradj(49). The Smen_Node →            |
   | Bile_Acid_Secretion edge weight here is HIGH given the Kila measure. Patients  |
   | on Cyclosporine post-transplant should be aware of the Cmax amplification,      |
   | especially if consuming multiple pieces. Confidence: HIGH.                      |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Dual Nut Load / L-Arginine + Mg²⁺ / Vascular Node]:               |
   | Tcharak has a double nut load: filling INSIDE + crushed nut coat OUTSIDE. This  |
   | gives one of the higher per-serving Arginine + Magnesium densities in the       |
   | dataset — comparable to Shbah el-Safra (50). The Arginine → eNOS → NO pathway  |
   | provides mild vasodilatory support, additive with ACE inhibitors and            |
   | theoretically problematic in combination with Sildenafil or organic nitrates.   |
   | Mg²⁺ independently supports Ca-channel blockade. Together these make Tcharak   |
   | the most “cardio-supportive” crescent in the dataset despite its lipid load.    |
   | GCN: VARIANT-CONDITIONAL edge on Arginine_NO and Mg_Vasodilation based on nut  |
   | type. Confidence: MEDIUM-HIGH.                                                  |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Orange Blossom Soaking — Meskar / Highest Linalool Concentration]: |
   | In Meskar, the fully baked crescents are soaked in orange blossom water (not    |
   | just garnished), delivering a substantially higher Linalool dose than any       |
   | comparable dish. This makes Meskar the highest per-serving Linalool-per-gram   |
   | entry in the floral-water group (Bradj/49, Makroud/34, Shbah/50, Baklawa/33). |
   | The GABA-A potentiation signal with benzodiazepines is therefore stronger in    |
   | Meskar than in any garnish-only flower-water dish. Limonene from Aryan’s lemon |
   | zest adds mild CYP3A4 induction and digestive carminative effect helping        |
   | process the heavy Smen load. Confidence: MEDIUM (linalool soaking dose          |
   | meaningful; CYP3A4 induction minor at zest quantity).                          |
   |                                                                               |
   | GCN POSITION: Tcharak = FRIABLE-MATRIX node (shared characteristic with        |
   | Makroud-34 Non-Cohesive Matrix; Tcharak has higher Smen ratio = stronger fat    |
   | barrier); B=Meskar adds UNIQUE Sucrose_Dust rapid-burst node (fastest mouth-   |
   | dissolution sugar delivery in dataset); Dual_Nut node (inside+outside) shared  |
   | conceptually with Baklawa(33) but smaller scale; Linalool node: Meskar = 5th   |
   | floral-water dish AND highest soaking concentration; Smen_Bile_Activation shared|
   | with Makroud(34), Bradj(49), Mbessas(47), Lahm Lhalou(06).                     |
   | VARIANT-CONDITIONAL matrix: A=Aryan (slow-only, lemon-CYP3A4) vs B=Meskar      |
   | (biphasic-sucrose + high-Linalool-soak) — two pharmacologically distinct        |
   | profiles in one dish number.                                                    |
   |                                                                               |

   |                           | SMILES: OP(=O)(O)OC1C(OP    | antibiotic / mineral

   | Interaction [Purines / Tripe]: Douwara uses offal (tripe = stomach lining),    |
   | which carries organ-grade nucleic acid density. Stewing concentrates purines  |
   | into the broth itself — even the liquid is a purine load. Strong antagonistic  |
   | edge against Allopurinol/Febuxostat: xanthine oxidase inhibitors overwhelmed  |
   | → acute gout flare triggered even in a stable patient. Confidence: HIGH.      |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Lycopene + Flavonoids / Red Sauce]: Tomato-based red sauce —     |
   | lycopene bioavailability is amplified by the fat content of the stew. High    |
   | dietary lycopene intake correlates with enhanced anti-platelet activity        |
   | (synergistic with Warfarin and Aspirin) → elevated INR / bleeding time.       |
   | This is the same Lycopene node active in dishes 03, 09, 13, 16. Confidence: HIGH.|
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [K⁺ / Chickpeas]: Chickpeas are a high-potassium legume. Combined |
   | with ACE inhibitors (which reduce aldosterone-driven K⁺ excretion) or K⁺-      |

   | → cardiac arrhythmia risk. Confidence: HIGH.                                  |

   | Interaction [Phytic Acid / Chickpeas]: Chickpeas provide the primary Phytic   |
   | Acid chelation node in this dish (same as Doubara dish 19, Garantita dish 21).|
   | Chelation of Ciprofloxacin and Iron Supplements in the gut → reduced          |
   | absorption. Confidence: HIGH.                                                 |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Allicin + Gingerol + Curcumin / Spice Base]: The triple-allium   |

   | modulation node: Allicin (CYP3A4/CYP2C9 modulator), Gingerol (P-gp           |
   | inhibitor), Curcumin (CYP3A4 inhibitor). Together these cause unpredictable   |
   | fluctuations in plasma levels of Statins, HIV protease inhibitors, and        |

   |                                                                               |
   | NOTE: Douwara is a Ramadan and wedding feast staple. The offal-stew context    |
   | means patients will likely have taken multiple medications before eating.      |
   | The purine load + K⁺ elevation + anticoagulant potentiation in a single bowl  |
   | creates a multi-pathway risk cluster unique in the offal-dish group.          |
   | Compare: Bouzlouf (dish 18, max Allicin), Douwara (max K⁺ + Purine + Lycopene  |
   | convergence). Both are offal-based but have distinct molecular profiles.      |

   | Interaction [NaHCO₃ / Bicarbonate Soak]: Chickpeas are soaked 24h in          |
   | bicarbonate water to accelerate softening. Residual NaHCO₃ in the broth is    |
   | an alkalizing agent → raises gastric pH. Enteric-coated tablets are designed  |
   | to resist dissolution at low gastric pH (pH<3) but dissolve at intestinal pH  |
   | (pH>6). A bicarbonate-rich meal can pre-empt dissolution in the stomach →     |
   | drug released too early → degraded by gastric enzymes or causes gastric       |
   | irritation (Aspirin, NSAIDs) or reduced absorption (Omeprazole, Diclofenac). |
   | Confidence: MEDIUM-HIGH.                                                      |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Phytic Acid / Melted Chickpea Broth]: Unlike whole chickpea      |
   | dishes, the Hommos process fully dissolves chickpeas into the broth,   |
   | releasing Phytic Acid into a hot, stirred, 6L aqueous medium. This maximises  |
   | its chelation surface area — free IP6 molecules in solution can trap metallic  |
   | drugs far more efficiently than IP6 still bound inside whole legume cell walls.|
   | This is the highest-bioavailability Phytic Acid chelation scenario in the     |
   | dataset. Ciprofloxacin taken at meal time has near-zero absorption.           |
   | Confidence: HIGH.                                                              |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Purines / Dual Source]: Both chickpeas AND rabbit/veal bone-     |
   | marrow contribute nucleotides to the broth. Bone-marrow simmering releases    |
   | DNA/RNA from nucleated marrow cells into the stock. Combined with the         |
   | chickpea purine load = dual-source spike in Uric Acid production. Annotated  |
   | as a "Purine Bomb" — Allopurinol/Febuxostat overwhelmed. Confidence: HIGH.   |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Cuminaldehyde / Double-Dose]: Cumin added during cooking AND     |
   | sprinkled fresh as table garnish = maximum cuminaldhyde exposure. CYP2C9       |
   | inhibition → Warfarin accumulates → bleeding risk; Glipizide accumulates →   |
   | hypoglycaemia. Same double-dose pattern as Garantita (dish 21).              |
   | Confidence: HIGH.                                                              |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Fats / Olive Oil + Bone Fat]: Finishing oil and rendered bone    |
   | fat create a lipid vehicle elevating absorption of fat-soluble drugs, similar  |
   | to the Mederbel (dish 24) lipid-sponge effect, but via olive oil rather than  |
   | deep-frying. Confidence: MEDIUM-HIGH.                                         |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Non-Heme Iron / 1kg Chickpeas]: 1kg of chickpeas contributes     |
   | approximately 60mg of dietary iron — one of the highest single-dish iron loads |
   | in the dataset. In the hot broth, Fe²⁺/Fe³⁺ ions are free in solution and bind   |
   | directly to Levothyroxine and Bisphosphonates in the stomach → insoluble       |
   | complexes → not absorbed → “Absorption Wall.” Thyroid patients eating this     |
   | soup regularly will see TSH levels drift upward (under-medication signal)     |
   | despite taking their dose. Dose separation ≥2h recommended. Confidence: HIGH. |
   |                                                                               |
   | IRON PARADOX — Bicarbonate-Iron Conflict: Hommos contains both a       |
   | massive Non-Heme Iron load (60mg from chickpeas) AND residual NaHCO₃ from the  |
   | 24h soak. Non-heme iron absorption requires a highly acidic gastric environment|
   | (pH<3 for Fe³⁺ → Fe²⁺ reduction). Bicarbonate neutralises gastric acid → raises  |
   | pH → non-heme iron remains as Fe(OH)₃ precipitate → not absorbed. The soup   |
   | is simultaneously iron-rich AND iron-absorption-inhibiting. A patient with    |
   | anaemia eating this dish may show no improvement in iron status despite the   |
   | high iron content. This is the same Iron Paradox annotated in Garantita       |
   | (dish 21) but inverted: here bicarbonate (not Phytic Acid) is the mechanism.  |
   | GCN: Add NEGATIVE-WEIGHT edge from NaHCO₃ node → Iron_Absorption node.       |
   | Confidence: HIGH.                                                              |
   |                                                                               |
   | NOTE: Hommos is the HIGHEST Phytic Acid chelation-efficiency dish in   |
   | the dataset due to full chickpea dissolution into broth. Any metallic drug    |
   | (Fluoroquinolones, Iron, Zinc, Bisphosphonates) co-administered at this       |
   | meal is at severe risk of complete absorption failure.                        |
   | The NaHCO₃ + Phytic Acid + Non-Heme Iron triple-node combination is unique:  |
   | bicarbonate raises pH, Phytic Acid chelates free metals, and the broth is     |
   | simultaneously high in iron that cannot be absorbed — a three-way paradox.   |
   |                                                                               |
   | GCN Actions:                                                                  |
   |   - NaHCO₃ / Bicarbonate: new node; ALKALINE-pH edge → Enteric-Coated-Meds; |
   |     annotate premature-dissolution property; add NEGATIVE-WEIGHT edge →     |
   |     Iron_Absorption (bicarbonate-iron conflict).                             |
   |   - Phytic Acid [✓]: shared node; annotate DISSOLVED-BROTH = highest surface |
   |     area; confirm Ciprofloxacin + Iron + Zinc edges.                         |
   |   - Purines [✓]: shared node; annotate DUAL-SOURCE (legume + bone marrow);  |
   |     confirm Allopurinol + Febuxostat edges.                                  |
   |   - Cuminaldehyde: shared node (18/19/21/22/39); double-dose annotation;    |
   |     confirm CYP2C9 + Warfarin + Glipizide edges.                            |
   |   - Oleic Acid / Dietary Fat: shared lipid node; confirm Statin Cmax edges. |

   |     annotate IRON PARADOX with NaHCO₃ negative-weight counter-edge.         |

   | Interaction [Ca²⁺ / Klila]: Klila is a concentrated dried fermented cheese     |
   | with exceptionally high calcium density. In the stomach, Ca²⁺ ions chelate   |
   | Tetracyclines (Doxycycline, Tetracycline) and Bisphosphonates (Alendronate,   |
   | Risedronate) → insoluble metal-drug complexes → excreted without absorption.  |
   | For Tetracyclines: clinical outcome is treatment failure (antibiotic not       |
   | active). For Bisphosphonates: osteoporosis medication is inactivated. Patients |
   | should separate Klila-containing meals from these drugs by ≥2 hours.           |
   | GCN: STRONG INHIBITORY (CHELATION) edge, higher weight than plant-Ca²⁺ sources |
   | due to concentrated dairy calcium density. Confidence: HIGH.                  |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Beta-Glucans / Triple-Steamed Dchicha]: Dchicha is cracked        |
   | barley. The traditional triple-steaming (Tafwir) process gelatinizes the      |
   | starches and makes soluble Beta-Glucan maximally accessible — more so than     |
   | single-cooked barley. The resulting gel matrix dramatically increases gastric  |
   | viscosity → slows intestinal transit time → delayed Tmax for ALL oral        |
   | medications. Same mechanism as Harira Mermez/barley (dish 25) but amplified   |
   | by the triple-steaming gelatinisation step. Confidence: HIGH.                 |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Tyramine / Klila Fermentation]: During the drying and             |
   | fermentation of Klila, bacterial decarboxylation of tyrosine → Tyramine       |
   | accumulation. In patients on MAOIs (used for depression/anxiety), dietary      |
   | Tyramine triggers massive noradrenaline release (MAOIs block Tyramine          |
   | degradation) → “Cheese Effect” → hypertensive crisis, potentially fatal.    |
   | Same Tyramine node as Guedid (dish 04). Confidence: MEDIUM-HIGH.              |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Sodium / Jumbo Bouillon]: The recipe uses a Jumbo bouillon cube   |
   | as the primary seasoning stock. Bouillon cubes are extremely high in sodium    |
   | (typically 900–1100mg Na⁺ per cube). High dietary sodium directly opposes     |
   | antihypertensive medications (ACE inhibitors, ARBs, diuretics) → reduced BP  |
   | control. Annotated alongside Tyramine as co-resident cardiovascular risk node.|
   | Confidence: MEDIUM-HIGH.                                                      |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Saturated Fats / Dehan-Smen]: Aged clarified butter (Dehan) is   |
   | added at the end as a finishing fat. High lipid bolus → bile acid release →   |
   | elevated solubility of fat-soluble medications → Cmax spike. Same mechanism   |
   | as Smen/Sh’ham in dishes 13/14/17/18/22. Isotretinoin patients are highest     |
   | risk (requires fat for absorption — overdose risk with large fat bolus).       |
   | Confidence: HIGH.                                                              |
   |                                                                               |
   | NOTE: Dchicha with Klila is the only dish in the dataset with a DAIRY CALCIUM  |
   | chelation node (Klila). Dairy Ca²⁺ produces a stronger chelation edge than    |
   | plant Ca²⁺ sources because dairy products deliver ionised Ca²⁺ at high         |
   | concentration directly in solution. GCN should assign higher edge weight to   |
   | this Ca²⁺-Tetracycline edge than equivalent plant-source calcium dishes.       |
   | The Beta-Glucan Tmax delay + Ca²⁺ chelation + Tyramine crisis = three distinct  |
   | high-severity pathways from a single dish.                                    |
   |                                                                               |
   | GCN Actions:                                                                  |
   |   - Calcium (Ca²⁺): new node; STRONG CHELATION edge → Tetracyclines +        |
   |     Bisphosphonates; annotate DAIRY-CALCIUM = higher edge weight.            |
   |   - Beta-Glucan: shared node (25/41); annotate TRIPLE-STEAMED → amplified   |
   |     Tmax-delay vs. single-cook barley; confirm all-oral-meds delayed edge.   |
   |   - Tyramine: shared node (04/41); confirm MAOI HYPERTENSIVE-CRISIS edge.   |
   |   - Sodium (Na⁺): shared node (04/41); confirm antihypertensive-antagonism. |
   |   - Saturated Fats / Dehan: shared Smen node; confirm Statin + Isotretinoin  |
   |     Cmax edges.                                                               |
   | Source: Neuvonen PJ, Clin Pharmacokinet 1976 (Ca²⁺/Tetracycline chelation);  |
   |         Wood CD, Aliment Pharmacol Ther 1994 (Beta-glucan/Tmax);            |
   |         McCabe-Sellers BJ, Am Diet Assoc 2006 (Tyramine/MAOI)              |
   |                                                                               |
