   | Interaction [Capsaicin / Hot Peppers]: Roasting concentrates capsaicin in the  |
   | pepper skin. Capsaicin sensitises Substance P nociceptive fibres in the        |
   | respiratory tract → amplifies the dry cough that is an established side        |
   | effect of ACE inhibitors (bradykinin + Substance P mechanism). Patients on     |
   | Lisinopril or Enalapril who eat Hmiss before or during their dose may report  |
   | severe, persistent cough. Theophylline (CYP1A2) also affected.                |
   | Confidence: HIGH.                                                              |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Vitamin C + Flavonoids / Sweet Peppers]: Sweet peppers are        |
   | among the highest dietary sources of Ascorbic Acid. Ascorbic Acid (Vitamin C)  |
   | reduces Fe³⁺ to Fe²⁺ in the gut, the form that non-heme iron transporters      |
   | preferentially absorb. This is an ABSORPTION SYNERGY edge — the opposite of   |
   | chelation nodes. Patients on Iron Supplements who eat Hmiss absorb 2–3× more  |
   | iron than expected from the supplement alone → risk of iron overload in high-  |
   | dose supplementation. Unique counter-node: Hmiss contains both Fe-absorption  |
   | ENHANCER (Vit C) and Fe-absorption INHIBITOR (Allicin mild effect). GCN should |
   | annotate net-positive edge to Iron node. Confidence: HIGH.                    |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Lycopene / Roasted Tomato in Oil]: Heat processing releases       |
   | lycopene from plant cell matrices and isomerises trans-lycopene to cis-lycopene|
   | (higher bioavailability). Olive oil co-present amplifies lipophilic absorption. |
   | Higher cis-lycopene blood levels correlate with mild vasodilatory / antioxidant |
   | activity → additive hypotension with prescription antihypertensives.           |
   | Same Lycopene node as dishes 03/09/13/16/24/38. Confidence: MEDIUM-HIGH.      |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Allicin / Sautéed Garlic]: Moderate sautéeing preserves a         |
   | significant Allicin fraction (partial alliinase activity — less than late-     |
   | addition raw garlic in dish 26, more than long-fried garlic). Anti-platelet    |
   | and mild anticoagulant effects are additive with Warfarin, Aspirin, and        |
   | Clopidogrel. Confidence: HIGH.                                                 |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Oleic Acid / Olive Oil]: Olive oil is both the roasting medium    |
   | and the finishing dressing, creating a persistent dietary fat matrix. Bile acid |
   | release → elevated solubility of fat-soluble co-administered drugs. Effect     |
   | magnitude is moderate (less than deep-frying in dish 24, more than broth fat). |
   | Also potentiates lycopene bioavailability (fat-soluble carotenoid). GCN:       |
   | shared Oleic Acid node with dish 39. Confidence: MEDIUM-HIGH.                 |
   |                                                                               |
   | NOTE: Hmiss represents the IRON SYNERGY counter-node in the dataset. Most      |
   | other dishes reduce iron absorption (Phytic Acid, Tannins, Nasunin). Hmiss     |
   | is one of the few dishes where Vitamin C actively ENHANCES iron absorption —  |
   | forming a positive-weight edge to Iron that runs opposite to all chelation    |
   | edges. This makes it valuable for GCN training signal diversity.              |
   | Additionally Capsaicin + Allicin + Lycopene create a multi-pathway             |
   | cardiovascular risk cluster: cough potentiation + platelet inhibition +        |
   | mild hypotension — all three cardiovascular drug classes affected together.   |
   |                                                                               |
   | GCN Actions:                                                                  |
   |   - Capsaicin [✓]: shared node; confirm ACE-I Substance P edge; annotate    |
   |     roasted-concentration property.                                          |
   |   - Vitamin C / Ascorbic Acid: shared node; add ABSORPTION-SYNERGY (positive |
   |     weight) edge → Iron Supplements; annotate Fe³⁺→Fe²⁺ reduction mechanism.|
   |   - Lycopene [✓]: shared node (03/09/13/16/24/38/42); annotate HEAT+OIL =   |
   |     max cis-isomer bioavailability; confirm antihypertensive synergy.        |
   |   - Allicin [✓]: shared node; annotate MODERATE-SAUTE potency (between      |
   |     late-raw dish 26 and long-fried); confirm Warfarin/Aspirin edges.        |
   |   - Oleic Acid / Olive Oil: shared node (39/42); confirm Statin Cmax edges;  |
   |     annotate lycopene-bioavailability amplifier role.                        |
   | Source: Bhutani MK, Nat Rev Drug Discov 2007 (Capsaicin/ACE-I cough);        |

   |         Agarwal S, J Nutr 1998 (heat-processed lycopene bioavailability)    |

   | Interaction [Thin-Pastry Lipid Hub]: Malsouka (brick pastry / warka) is         |
   | significantly thinner than Bourak’s Dioul dough. The higher surface-area-to-    |
   | mass ratio means more dough surface contacts hot oil during frying, resulting   |
   | in a larger lipid bolus absorbed per gram of pastry. Bile acid secretion is     |
   | stimulated proportionally, amplifying bioavailability of co-administered        |
   | fat-soluble drugs (Statins, Cyclosporine, Isotretinoin, Vitamin D).             |
   | Confidence: HIGH.                                                              |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Whole Egg / Lecithin-Choline]: Brik’s defining feature vs. Bourak  |
   | is the whole egg cracked inside before frying. Cooking emulsifies the yolk      |
   | phosphatidylcholine into the oil-dough matrix. This Lecithin node raises bile   |
   | micelle capacity and increases Cmax of any lipophilic drug taken with the meal  |
   | (additive to the lipid-hub effect above). Free choline from the heated yolk    |
   | exerts mild PD antagonism at muscarinic receptor sites against anticholinergic  |
   | drugs. The egg node is ALWAYS present in Brik; this distinguishes it from       |
   | Bourak (dish 28) where egg is absent.                                          |
   | Confidence: MEDIUM-HIGH.                                                       |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Fried Mince Purines]: Deep-frying minced beef/lamb removes water  |
   | thermally, concentrating hypoxanthine and xanthine per gram above the levels   |
   | found in stewed or boiled meat of identical weight. The Purine node is ALWAYS  |
   | active (not a variant like Bourak dish 28). Concurrent Allopurinol or          |
   | Febuxostat use: meal can precipitate acute gout flare by spike in uric acid    |
   | precursors. Confidence: HIGH.                                                  |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Cuminaldehyde / Raw-Mince Mixing]: Cumin is added to the raw     |
   | mince before wrapping. Unlike long-cooked stews (low volatility) or late-added |
   | garlic (high potency), raw-mince mixing represents intermediate Cuminaldehyde  |
   | preservation. CYP2C9 inhibition is moderate: Warfarin plasma levels ↑ 15-25%  |
   | expected; Glipizide half-life extended. Confidence: HIGH.                      |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [High-SA Dough Acrylamide]: Thin Malsouka pastry has greater       |
   | Maillard surface area per gram than Bourak. Acrylamide generation at frying    |
   | temperature (>150°C) is proportionally higher. CYP2E1 induction is expected    |
   | to exceed the Bourak level, modestly accelerating metabolism of Acetaminophen  |
   | and Chlorzoxazone. Confidence: MEDIUM.                                         |
   |                                                                               |

   | Interaction [Amylopectin / Rapid Glycaemic Flatbread]: Mhadjeb dough is kneaded|
   | from fine semolina and stretched to a very thin crepe on a hot griddle. The    |
   | thinness maximises heat penetration and starch gelatinisation speed. This      |
   | produces among the fastest glucose-release profiles of any flatbread in this   |
   | dataset. Co-administration of Metformin, Insulin, or Acarbose: glycaemic spike |
   | may overwhelm dose-adjusted glucose control. Confidence: HIGH.                 |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Lycopene / Synergistic Hypotension]: Sautéed tomato filling cooked|
   | in oil generates cis-lycopene with maximum vascular bioavailability (same      |
   | mechanism as dish 42 Hmiss). Combined with antihypertensive drugs, additive    |
   | blood pressure reduction may cause symptomatic hypotension, especially in      |
   | Ramadan evening meals (fasted state, rapid food intake). Confidence: MEDIUM.   |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Allicin + Quercetin / Anticoagulant Bleeding Risk]: Garlic cooked |
   | as part of the filling undergoes moderate-heat sautéing; Allicin preservation  |
   | is intermediate (less than late-addition dish 26, more than long-stewed dishes).|
   | Quercetin from onion inhibits platelet aggregation independently. Both nodes    |
   | act additively with oral anticoagulants (Warfarin, Clopidogrel, Aspirin).      |
   | Confidence: HIGH.                                                              |
   |                                                                               |

   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Triglycerides / Surface Oil Lipid Node]: Oil used in griddle      |

   | stimulation is present but modest. Cmax amplification for fat-soluble drugs    |

   | Confidence: MEDIUM.                                                            |

   |                           |                              | ABSENT if no butter)
   |                           |                              |
   | Interaction [Amylopectin / Pure Glycaemic Flatbread]: Baghrir is unique in      |
   | this dataset for having NO egg (Lecithin/Cholesterol nodes absent), so the     |
   | GCN profile is focused entirely on starch kinetics and osmotic balance. The    |
   | yeast+baking powder leavening creates a honeycomb of fine pores that maximise  |
   | starch surface area beyond any other flatbread in the dataset. Glucose release |
   | is near-instant. For diabetics using Metformin or Acarbose, the glycaemic      |
   | challenge is severe. For insulin-dependent patients, rapid Tmax of glucose     |
   | requires precise meal-time insulin synchronisation. Confidence: HIGH.          |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [CO₂ / Gastric Distension + Prokinetics]: The honeycomb structure  |
   | is structurally maintained CO₂ gas. Ingestion of Baghrir releases this gas    |
   | in the stomach, causing rapid gastric distension. In patients taking            |
   | Metoclopramide or Domperidone (prokinetics), gastric wall stretch signals may  |
   | be altered or the prokinetic response overwhelmed. IBS patients on             |
   | prokinetics should be advised of this mechanistic interaction. This CO₂ node   |
   | is unique in the dataset — no other dish has a gas-phase interaction.          |
   | Confidence: MEDIUM.                                                            |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Honey / SGLT2 Osmotic Overlap — OPTIONAL NODE]: When honey is     |
   | applied, the fructose/glucose load is superimposed on the already-rapid starch |
   | spike. SGLT2 inhibitors (Empagliflozin, Dapagliflozin, Canagliflozin) work by  |
   | osmotic glucosuria — adding high dietary sugar + porous starch simultaneously  |
   | creates a compounded osmotic challenge to GI fluid balance. Risk of dehydration|
   | or electrolyte disturbance is elevated. GCN annotation: VARIANT-CONDITIONAL    |
   | edge (Honey node active only when garnish applied). Confidence: MEDIUM.        |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Butter / Bile Acid Sequestrant Bypass]: Short and medium chain    |
   | fatty acids (C4–C14) present in melted butter are absorbed directly via portal |
   | vein without obligate bile acid micellisation. They therefore partially bypass |
   | (compete with) the binding capacity of bile acid sequestrant drugs             |
   | (Cholestyramine, Colestipol). This reduces the effective dose of the           |
   | sequestrant active at its GI target. Node is ABSENT when Baghrir is served dry |
   | (no butter). Confidence: MEDIUM.                                               |
   |                                                                               |
   | KEY GCN NOTE — Baghrir vs. Standard Baghrir:                          |
   |   Egg-containing Baghrir (if encountered) would add: Lecithin node            |
   |   (fat-soluble drug Cmax ↑), Cholesterol node, Choline node.                  |
   |   Eggless version: these nodes ABSENT — GCN graph is a pure glycaemic +       |
   |   osmotic + gas-phase profile. Most distinctive single-dish GCN fingerprint   |
   |   in this dataset.                                                            |
   |   CO₂ gas node = UNIQUE to dish 45 in this dataset.                           |
   |                                                                               |
   | Source: Ludwig DS, JAMA 2002 (Glycaemic index fine semolina/porous starch);    |
   |         Nguyen NQ, Clin Gastroenterol Hepatol 2008 (Gastric distension/        |
   |         prokinetics/CO₂); Abumweis SS, J Am Diet Assoc 2010 (SCFA bile acid);|
   |         Scheen AJ, Lancet Diabetes Endocrinol 2015 (SGLT2/osmotic diuresis)   |
   |                                                                               |

   | Interaction [Partially Hydrolysed Starch / Glycaemic Velocity]: Yeast           |
   | fermentation in Matlouh activates both exogenous yeast amylases and endogenous  |
   | grain amylases during the proofing period. Simple sugars liberated during       |
   | fermentation are immediately available; remaining starch gelatinises quickly    |
   | on the hot griddle. The resulting glycaemic velocity is intermediate: faster   |
   | than dense unleavened Kesra, slower than the honeycomb-porous Baghrir (dish   |
   | 45). GCN edge weight for Glucose_Spike: MODERATE-HIGH. Patients on Insulin or  |
   | Sulfonylureas (Glibenclamide, Glipizide) should synchronise dose timing.        |
   | Confidence: HIGH.                                                              |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [CO₂ / Gastric Volume + Antacid/PPI Efficacy]: Like Baghrir, the   |
   | leavened crumb releases CO₂ on contact with gastric acid and mechanical        |
   | disruption. This adds gas volume to the stomach, increasing distension. For     |
   | patients dependent on PPIs for acid control, rapid gastric expansion may        |
   | transiently dilute gastric acid concentration before the PPI achieves full      |
   | suppression. The effect is milder than Baghrir (less porous) but present.      |
   | Confidence: MEDIUM.                                                            |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Hydration Matrix / Drug Carrier Effect]: The moist internal crumb  |
   | of freshly cooked Matlouh (عجينة طرية) acts as a physical matrix that can       |
   | absorb and briefly retain co-ingested medications. For acid-sensitive drugs     |
   | (e.g. enteric-coated tablets, certain antibiotics), this matrix may buffer      |
   | initial acid exposure before gastric emptying. GCN annotation: SYNERGISTIC     |
   | edge from Matlouh_Matrix to Drug_Protection for acid-labile compounds.          |
   | Confidence: MEDIUM (mechanism pharmacokinetically plausible, limited           |
   | direct clinical trial data for bread matrix specifically).                     |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Na⁺ / Diuretics & BP Meds]: Salt is incorporated into the dough   |
   | at standard flatbread levels. The porous crumb accelerates mucosal contact     |
   | vs. dense breads, giving slightly faster Na⁺ absorption. In patients on loop   |
   | diuretics (Furosemide) or ACE inhibitors where sodium balance is tightly        |
   | managed, this is a relevant dietary sodium source. Confidence: MEDIUM.         |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Reduced Phytic Acid / PROTECTIVE NEGATIVE-WEIGHT EDGE]: The       |
   | extended fermentation period activates grain phytase, hydrolysing phytate into |
   | inositol phosphates with lower chelation capacity. This is the opposite of      |
   | most dishes in this dataset. In GCN terms, this is a NEGATIVE-WEIGHT edge      |
   | from Phytic_Acid node toward Chelation_Risk. Matlouh is safer than Kesra,       |
   | Couscous (dish 01), or Garantita (dish 21) for patients taking Ciprofloxacin,  |
   | Levothyroxine, or mineral supplements. This protective node is shared with     |
   | Baghrir (dish 45) and Sourdough-type breads.                                  |
   | Confidence: HIGH (phytase activation by yeast/fermentation well established).  |
   |                                                                               |
   | GCN POSITION — Flatbread Glycaemic Spectrum (slowest → fastest glucose):      |
   |   Dense Kesra (stub) < Matlouh (dish 46) < Mhadjeb (dish 44)                 |
   |   < Baghrir (dish 45, porous honeycomb = fastest)                            |
   | CO₂ node shared with Baghrir (45/46); Phytase-reduction node unique to        |
   | fermented dishes (45/46) vs. unfermented flatbreads.                          |
   |                                                                               |

   |                           |                              |
   | Interaction [Bess Technique / Lipid-Encapsulated Starch]: The defining step of  |
   | Mbessas preparation is تبسيس (tbssis) — rubbing dry semolina with melted butter  |
   | before any water is added. This physically coats each starch granule in a       |
   | hydrophobic lipid layer before cooking. When consumed, alpha-amylase must       |
   | penetrate this lipid barrier before accessing the amylopectin chains,           |
   | dramatically reducing enzymatic access rate. This makes Mbessas the            |
   | LOWEST glycaemic velocity starch dish in this dataset. GCN edge weight for      |
   | Glucose_Spike: LOW. Patients on Metformin or Insulin: meal is one of the safest |
   | Algerian starch sources, though total carbohydrate load remains high.           |
   | Confidence: HIGH (lipid-starch encapsulation mechanism well established).       |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Butter / Bile Acid Maximiser & Lipophilic Drug Cmax]: With 1 cup  |
   | of melted butter per recipe, Mbessas delivers one of the highest fat boluses   |
   | of any dish in this dataset (comparable to deep-fried dishes 28/43 but via     |
   | griddle). This potently stimulates bile acid secretion, maximising micellar     |
   | solubilisation of fat-soluble co-administered drugs. Atorvastatin Cmax ↑~2x,   |
   | Griseofulvin bioavailability ↑~50% (known food-effect with high-fat meals),    |
   | Vitamin D, Isotretinoin, and Cyclosporine absorption all significantly          |
   | elevated. GCN annotation: HIGH-BIOAVAIL edge from Butter_CFA node to all       |
   | lipophilic drug nodes. Confidence: HIGH.                                       |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Dense Matrix / Delayed Gastric Emptying — Sahur Flag]: The low-   |
   | hydration, fat-saturated dough produces a compact, dense cake with minimal gas |
   | pockets. The fat+dense matrix combination is the strongest gastric-emptying    |
   | delay signal in this dataset — greater than buttered Baghrir, greater than     |
   | Dchicha (dish 41). All oral medications taken at the same meal will experience  |
   | extended Tmax. This is clinically significant for: time-sensitive drugs         |
   | (Levodopa, Levothyroxine, morning Metformin), enteric-coated tablets (prolonged |
   | retention in acid). SAHUR FLAG: Mbessas is a classic pre-dawn Ramadan food;    |
   | patients taking once-daily drugs at Sahur should expect substantially delayed  |
   | absorption. Confidence: HIGH.                                                  |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Maillard Crust / Trace Acrylamide + AGEs]: The 1 tsp sugar +      |
   | semolina protein browning on the hot Tajine generates trace acrylamide at the  |
   | crust surface (lower than deep-fried dishes due to moderate griddle temp and   |
   | short crust contact time). The primary Maillard output is the sustained        |
   | “long-burn” energy profile — dense calories from fat+starch = prolonged satiety|
   | relevant to Sahur energy modelling. Sucrose at 1 tsp across a full dish is      |
   | negligible for glycaemic impact. Confidence: MEDIUM (trace acrylamide).        |
   |                                                                               |
   | GCN POSITION — Flatbread Glycaemic Spectrum (slowest → fastest):              |
   |   Mbessas (47, lipid-encapsulated) < Dense Kesra (stub) < Matlouh (46)        |
   |   < Mhadjeb (44) < Baghrir (45, porous honeycomb = fastest)                  |
   | Butter node shared with: Lahm Lhalou (06), Osban (05), Trida (13W), Mesfouf   |
   | (14), Dchicha (41); Mbessas has highest butter-to-dough ratio in dataset.      |
   | UNIQUE node: Lipid-Encapsulated-Starch (tbssis technique) — only dish 47.      |

   | Interaction [Dense Unleavened Starch / Low-Moderate Glycaemic Velocity]: Ftir   |
   | is kneaded from fine semolina with only 50ml oil (no butter block, no yeast     |
   | pre-digestion). The compact, pore-free crumb means alpha-amylase must work      |

   | 1-cup butter or deep-fried dishes 28/43). Co-administered Statins, Vitamin D,  |
   | or Isotretinoin will see a modest Cmax increase. Confidence: MEDIUM-HIGH.      |
   |                                                                               |

   | standard home-baking level (approx. 1–2g NaCl per kg semolina). Combined with  |
   | no yeast-extract sodium contribution, Ftir is one of the lower-sodium          |

   | Interaction [K⁺ / Hyperkalemia — Date Paste Filling]: Ghers (compressed date    |
   | paste) is the densest potassium source in this dataset. Each piece of Bradj     |
   | contains a concentrated date-paste core. When consumed with ACE Inhibitors     |
   | (which retain K⁺ by blocking aldosterone) or K⁺-sparing diuretics             |
   | (Spironolactone, Amiloride), the combined K⁺ load can push serum potassium     |
   | toward hyperkalemia (>5.5 mmol/L). Risk: cardiac arrhythmia (peaked T-waves,  |
   | wide QRS, ventricular fibrillation at severe levels). Multiple pieces of Bradj |
   | at tea-time are clinically significant. GCN: HIGH-POSITIVE weight on K⁺ node. |
   | Confidence: HIGH.                                                              |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Triple Lipid Vector / Fat-Soluble Drug Cmax]: Smen + butter +     |
   | vegetable oil is the only triple-fat combination in this dataset. Each         |
   | contributes differently: Smen (fermented butter) adds short-chain fermentation |
   | products plus SCFA; fresh butter adds C14-C18 saturated fats; vegetable oil    |
   | adds unsaturated C18:1/C18:2. The combined fat mass is comparable to Mbessas  |
   | (dish 47) in bile-stimulating effect. Lipophilic antipsychotics (Haloperidol,  |
   | Quetiapine, Olanzapine) are particularly relevant given the Ramadan context    |
   | — these are often dosed at evening meal. Confidence: HIGH.                    |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Cinnamaldehyde / Dual Role — CYP2C9 Inhibition + Glucose          |
   | Modulation]: Cinnamon in Bradj serves two simultaneous pharmacological roles.  |
   | (1) CYP2C9 inhibition: Warfarin plasma levels elevated; Glipizide half-life    |
   | extended. (2) Negative modulator of glucose spike: Cinnamaldehyde improves     |
   | insulin sensitivity and slows postprandial glucose rise from date fructose.     |
   | GCN annotation: TWO edges from Cinnamaldehyde node — INHIBITION edge to        |
   | CYP2C9, and NEGATIVE-WEIGHT edge to Glucose_Spike. Confidence: HIGH.           |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Eugenol / Additive CYP2C9 Inhibition — Double-Spice Risk]:        |
   | Cloves add Eugenol, which independently inhibits CYP2C9 via the same           |
   | mechanism as Cinnamaldehyde. Combined: Bradj is the only dish in this dataset  |
   | with TWO CYP2C9 inhibitors acting simultaneously (Cinnamaldehyde + Eugenol).   |
   | The additive inhibition significantly exceeds either spice alone. Eugenol also  |
   | inhibits COX-1 → mild antiplatelet activity, additive with Aspirin/Clopidogrel.|
   | Patients on Warfarin consuming Bradj at Eid/tea-time: HIGH bleeding risk.      |
   | Confidence: HIGH.                                                              |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Omega-3/Lignans / Platelet Modulation + Hormone Pathway]:         |
   | Walnuts (ALA omega-3 → reduces TXA₂-mediated platelet aggregation) and sesame |
   | seeds (sesamin lignan → CYP2D6 inhibition + mild ERβ agonism) add two further |
   | pharmacological signals. Combined with Eugenol’s COX-1 inhibition: Bradj has  |
   | THREE converging antiplatelet signals (ALA + Eugenol + Cinnamaldehyde’s mild   |
   | platelet effect). Sesamin CYP2D6 inhibition slows Codeine→Morphine conversion |
   | and Tamoxifen activation. Sesamin phyto-oestrogen activity is additive with    |
   | HRT or antagonistic to Tamoxifen’s receptor binding. Confidence: MEDIUM-HIGH. |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Linalool / Mild GABA-A Synergy]: Orange blossom water (Ma’ el     |
   | Zahra) is used in small quantities but Linalool is volatile and rapidly        |
   | absorbed via GI mucosa. At typical Bradj serving quantities, the GABA-A        |
   | modulatory effect is mild but additive with benzodiazepines (Lorazepam,        |
   | Diazepam, Alprazolam) or other GABAergic medications. Most clinically          |
   | relevant in elderly patients or those on anxiolytic regimens. Confidence:      |
   | MEDIUM.                                                                        |
   |                                                                               |
   | UNIQUE NOTE — Double CYP2C9 Inhibitor: Bradj is the ONLY dish in this          |
   | dataset with two simultaneous CYP2C9 inhibitors (Cinnamaldehyde + Eugenol).   |
   | This represents the highest CYP2C9-mediated Warfarin accumulation risk of any  |
   | dish. GCN should annotate this with ADDITIVE-INHIBITION edge from the          |
   | combined spice node.                                                           |
   |                                                                               |

   |         Barceloux DG, Clin Toxicol 2009 (Eugenol/CYP2C9/Warfarin);           |
   |         Anderson RA, J Am Coll Nutr 2004 (Cinnamaldehyde/insulin sensitivity); |
   |         Coulman KD, J Nutr 2005 (Sesamin/CYP2D6/lignan/phytoestrogen);        |
   |         Perez-Jimenez J, Br J Nutr 2008 (ALA/walnut/platelet aggregation)     |

   | Interaction [Double-Fried Protein-Lipid Barrier / Slow-Release Node]:           |
   | The unique double-dip-double-fry process (تقليها للمرة الثانية) creates a two-  |
   | layer enclosure around each almond diamond: outer crust (oxidised frying oil    |
   | + denatured egg white) + inner egg-protein layer. This barrier has three GCN   |
   | effects simultaneously: (1) NEGATIVE-WEIGHT on Glycaemic_Index — protein/fat   |
   | shell slows alpha-amylase access to almond starch and delays syrup sugar        |
   | absorption; glycaemic velocity is MODERATE despite 250g sugar syrup; (2)       |
   | DELAYED-ABSORPTION on all co-administered lipophilic drugs (extended Tmax);    |
   | (3) Cmax-AMPLIFICATION for fat-soluble drugs from double bile-stimulation       |
   | (two lipid-rich layers). This is the only dish in the dataset with a node that  |
   | simultaneously retards glycaemia AND amplifies lipid drug bioavailability.      |
   | GCN label: DOUBLE-FRIED node = Protein_Lipid_Shell. Confidence: HIGH.          |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [L-Arginine / Nitric Oxide Vascular Synergy]:                      |
   | Almonds contain the highest L-Arginine density of any common food (~2.4g/100g).|
   | L-Arginine is the sole substrate for endothelial Nitric Oxide Synthase (eNOS). |
   | In patients on PDE5 inhibitors (Sildenafil, Tadalafil) — which work by         |
   | preventing cGMP breakdown downstream of NO — additional dietary L-Arginine     |
   | pushes more substrate through eNOS, increasing NO production and amplifying    |
   | vasodilation. At large servings (200g+ ground almonds), this is clinically      |
   | relevant. HIGH-dose organic nitrates (GTN, ISDN) work by the same NO pathway;  |
   | combining Sildenafil + Nitrates + high-Arginine almonds is the triple-risk       |
   | scenario for dangerous blood pressure drop. GCN edge: PD-SYNERGY from           |
   | L-Arginine_Node → NO_Production → Vascular_Tone (NEGATIVE-WEIGHT for blood     |
   | pressure). Confidence: MEDIUM-HIGH.                                            |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Saffron Crocin / SSRI Serotonergic Potentiation]:                 |
   | Crocin inhibits both serotonin and dopamine reuptake in vitro and in animal     |
   | models at doses equivalent to the saffron amounts used in this dish. Multiple   |
   | RCTs confirm Crocus sativus extract has clinical antidepressant effects         |
   | comparable to low-dose Fluoxetine. The combination of Crocin + SSRI represents |
   | additive serotonin pathway stimulation. While frank serotonin syndrome from a   |
   | single dish is unlikely, chronic daily consumption of saffron-rich sweets while |
   | on SSRIs warrants flagging. GCN: Positive_Modulator edge from Saffron_Crocin   |
   | → Neurotransmitter_Balance. SHARED NODE with Chabah Essafra (dish 36).         |
   | Confidence: MEDIUM (dose-dependent at typical pinch-of-saffron serving).        |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [K⁺ + Sorbitol / Dried Fruits / Renal & Osmotic Risk]:             |
   | Apricots (both fresh and dried) are one of the richest dietary potassium        |
   | sources. 100g dried apricots ≈ 1160mg K⁺; a generous serving in this           |
   | confection can add 400-700mg extra K⁺ alongside the syrup glucose/fructose     |
   | load. For patients on ACE inhibitors or K-sparing diuretics (Spironolactone),  |
   | this creates genuine hyperkalaemia risk. Sorbitol (natural sugar alcohol in     |
   | apricots) additionally creates osmotic GI flux, drawing water into the lumen   |
   | and accelerating transit — this shortens drug absorption window for enteric-    |
   | coated formulations. GCN: Osmotic_Pressure tag on Renal_Filtration node from   |
   | Dried_Fruit_K_Sorbitol. Confidence: HIGH (K⁺ interaction); MEDIUM (Sorbitol    |
   | transit effect at typical serving size).                                       |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Double-Egg Lecithin / Lipophilic Drug Cmax Amplification]:        |
   | Two rounds of full egg-coat and frying make Shbah el-Safra the highest         |
   | per-serving lecithin load in this dataset, exceeding single-egg preparations   |
   | in Brik (dish 43) and Zlabia (dish 32). The resulting bile secretion surge      |
   | from the double lipid bolus maximises micellar solubilisation of any co-ingested|
   | fat-soluble drug (Cyclosporine, Isotretinoin, fat-soluble statins, Amiodarone). |
   | Cmax can increase 1.5–2× vs fasted state for highly lipophilic medications.    |
   | GCN: Cmax-AMPLIFICATION + DELAYED-ABSORPTION (Tmax) from Protein_Lipid_Shell. |
   | Confidence: HIGH.                                                              |
   |                                                                               |
   | GCN POSITION: Shbah el-Safra = DOUBLE-FRIED node (unique in dataset — only     |
   | dish with double-egg-coat/fry cycle); Protein_Lipid_Shell = dual-function node |
   | (NEGATIVE-WEIGHT on glycaemia + Cmax-AMPLIFICATION for lipophilics — first    |
   | such ambivalent node in dataset). L-Arginine_NO_Vascular = unique node shared  |
   | only with high-almond dishes; Saffron_Crocin shared with Chabah Essafra (36); |
   | Linalool_GABA shared with Bradj (49); K⁺_ElectrolyteSpike shared with Bradj/   |
   | Loubia/Adas. RAMADAN FLAG: Quintessential Eid/wedding confection — large       |
   | repeated servings at celebrations; Cmax risk for lipophilic drugs most          |
   | relevant in post-feast context.                                                |

   | Interaction [Skin-On Peanut / Resveratrol + PAC Antiplatelet Node]:              |
   | Peanut skins (قشور الفول السودانية) contain the highest proanthocyanidin (PAC) density|
   | of any common legume tegument. PACs suppress platelet aggregation by inhibiting  |
   | TXA₂ synthesis (COX-1 pathway) and down-regulating P-selectin surface            |
   | expression. Resveratrol adds secondary COX-1 inhibition. Combined, these two    |
   | polyphenols from the peanut skin create an antiplatelet signal that is          |
   | additive with Aspirin (also COX-1) and Clopidogrel (ADP-P2Y₂ pathway —         |
   | independent, so fully additive). For patients on dual antiplatelet therapy,     |
   | regular Dziriyet consumption constitutes triple antiplatelet signalling.          |
   | GCN: Peanut_Skins → Vascular_Endothelium (Cardio-Protective tag) + Antiplatelet|
   | edge. Confidence: MEDIUM-HIGH (PAC/TXA₂ mechanism well established; dose at     |
   | typical skin-on peanut portion is meaningful).                                  |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Benzaldehyde Aroma / Hepatic Aldehyde Oxidase Signature]:          |
   | The use of عطر اللوز المر (bitter almond flavouring compound) introduces pure        |
   | Benzaldehyde — not Amygdalin. Benzaldehyde is metabolised via aldehyde oxidase  |
   | (AOX1) and MAO-B to benzoic acid, then glycine-conjugated to hippuric acid for  |
   | renal excretion. At culinary flavouring concentrations this is safe. However:   |
   | (1) patients with AOX1 genetic polymorphisms have reduced benzaldehyde           |
   | clearance; (2) patients on MAO-B inhibitors (Selegiline, Rasagiline for         |
   | Parkinson’s) — Benzaldehyde competes for the same enzyme, mildly extending     |
   | MAO-B inhibitor exposure. These are clinical edge-cases but represent a          |
   | biochemically unique node (Benzaldehyde) not present in any other dish in the   |
   | dataset. Confidence: LOW-MEDIUM (safe at aroma doses; flagged for completeness).|
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Albumin-Peanut Protein Matrix / Lower Glycaemic Index]:            |
   | The egg albumin + peanut protein co-structure in the 35g filling creates a      |
   | protein network that physically retards starch/sugar diffusion during digestion. |
   | This gives Dziriyet a LOWER effective glycaemic index than pure syrup pastries   |
   | (Zlabia, honey-only sweets) despite being fully soaked. The protein matrix      |
   | also acts as a carrier matrix for egg-yolk lecithin (Choline), amplifying       |
   | transport of co-ingested fat-soluble drugs. GCN: Protein_Egg_Matrix →          |
   | NEGATIVE-WEIGHT on Glycaemic_Index; Lecithin node shared with Brik(43),         |
   | Zlabia(32), Shbah el-Safra(50). Confidence: HIGH.                              |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Thermal Osmosis / Hot Pastry + Cold Honey — High Sugar Density]:   |
   | The instruction نعسلها وهي ساخنة والعسل بارد (honey while hot, honey cold) exploits       |
   | thermodynamic capillary osmosis: the hot, porous peanut-egg centre has          |
   | expanded pores; cold, viscous honey is drawn inward by both pressure gradient   |
   | and thermal contraction as the pastry cools. The result is complete saturation  |
   | of the 35g core. This means the “small tartlet” perception is deceptive — the  |
   | sugar-per-gram density equals or exceeds larger pastries. For a diabetic        |
   | patient consuming 3-4 Dziriyet, the total glucose load is comparable to 1-2      |
   | pieces of Baklawa. GCN: Thermal_Osmosis = unique process node (shared technique |
   | concept with Zlabia’s hot-pore honey soaking, but applied differently). This    |
   | node represents MODERATE-HIGH glycaemic load per gram — the highest sugar       |
   | concentration efficiency of any tartlet-scale dish in the dataset. Confidence: |
   | HIGH.                                                                           |
   |                                                                               |
   | GCN POSITION: Dziriyet = SKIN-ON PEANUT node (unique — only dish in dataset with |
   | peanut skin PACs + Resveratrol as primary polyphenol node; all other dishes     |
   | use either skinless nuts or different polyphenol sources); Benzaldehyde =       |
   | dataset-unique hepatic flavour node (no other dish has AOX1/MAO-B occupancy);  |
   | Thermal_Osmosis shared conceptually with Zlabia(32) hot-pore soaking but         |
   | distinct process; Protein_Egg_Matrix node shared attribute-type with Shbah(50); |
   | Lecithin/Choline shared with Brik(43), Zlabia(32), Shbah(50).                  |
   | Antiplatelet node intensity: PACs+Resveratrol from skin > walnut-ALA alone.     |
   | RAMADAN/Eid FLAG: served in large quantities at celebrations; multiple small     |
   | tartlets = cumulative sugar density easily underestimated.                       |
   |                                                                               |

   |         Charman WN, J Pharm Sci 1992 (Lecithin/lipophilic drug transport);     |

   | Interaction [2:1 Shortcrust / High-Fat Starch Encapsulation — Low Glycaemic    |
   | Velocity]: The 250g butter to 500g flour ratio makes Kaak Nakach the highest   |
   | fat-to-flour shortcrust in this dataset's biscuit-cookie subgroup (exceeding   |
   | Tcharak 35 in absolute butter content). Every starch granule is coated in a   |
   | dense butter lipid layer before and during baking. This creates the same       |
   | starch-encapsulation mechanism documented in Mbessas (tbssis), but achieved    |
   | via rubbing-in technique rather than direct Smen-coating. The result is a      |
   | LOW-MODERATE glycaemic velocity — despite the friable, melt-in-mouth texture   |
   | suggesting fast digestion, the intestinal phase starch hydrolysis is slow.     |
   | Position on spectrum: Makroud(34) ≈ Kaak Nakach(52) < Tcharak(35) < Zlabia(32).
   | Confidence: HIGH.                                                              |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Date Paste K⁺ / Hyperkalemia Risk with Antihypertensive Agents]:  |
   | Tmer (date paste) provides ~700mg K⁺/100g in dense filling form. Patients on  |
   | ACE inhibitors (Lisinopril, Ramipril, Enalapril) or K⁺-sparing diuretics      |
   | (Spironolactone, Amiloride) are at risk of hyperkalemia when consuming          |
   | multiple pieces. The insoluble fiber component additionally slows gut transit,  |
   | extending the K⁺ absorption window and potentially prolonging the K⁺ spike.   |
   | Clinical significance is MODERATE — lower than Bradj (49, which has triple    |
   | lipid + date paste combined) but comparable to the date-paste contribution in  |
   | Makroud. GCN: K⁺_Hyperkalemia edge from Date_Paste_K node (shared across      |
   | Bradj/Makroud/Kaak Nakach subgroup). Confidence: MEDIUM-HIGH.                 |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Linalool / GABA-A Mild Sedative Synergy]:                         |
   | Orange blossom water is used at garnish/flavouring level. Linalool is rapidly  |
   | absorbed via gastrointestinal mucosa and crosses the BBB, exerting mild        |
   | positive GABA-A modulation. The effect is additive with benzodiazepines and    |
   | other GABAergic drugs. Kaak Nakach joins the floral-water subgroup (Bradj 49,  |
   | Makroud 34, Tcharak B 35, Baklawa 33) as the 6th Linalool-carrying dish in    |
   | this dataset. At garnish-level concentration (not soaked, unlike Tcharak       |
   | Meskar), effect magnitude is LOW-MILD. Most relevant in elderly patients or    |
   | high-consumption scenarios (multiple pieces). Confidence: MEDIUM.              |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [NaHCO₃ Leavening / Gastric pH Modulation of Acidic Drug           |
   | Dissolution]: Chemical leavening (baking powder — primarily NaHCO₃) is added  |
   | in small amounts (1–2g/500g flour). During baking, it decomposes to CO₂ +     |
   | Na⁺ + H₂O, creating the crumbly texture. Any residual NaHCO₃ in the baked    |
   | product, when consumed, acts as a weak antacid — transiently raising gastric   |
   | pH. This is clinically low significance at typical serving sizes, but          |
   | mechanistically it can: (1) reduce dissolution of acidic drugs (Aspirin,       |
   | NSAIDs) in the gastric phase; (2) prematurely dissolve enteric coatings        |
   | (Omeprazole delayed-release, Mesalazine) if gastric pH rises above 5–6.        |
   | UNIQUE: Kaak Nakach is the only shortcrust cookie in this dataset with a       |
   | chemical leavening node (NaHCO₃). Hommos (dish 39) also carries an NaHCO₃    |
   | alkaline node but via a different mechanism (cooking soda). GCN annotation:    |
   | NEGATIVE-WEIGHT edge: NaHCO₃_Residual → Gastric_AcidDissolution (LOW         |
   | magnitude). Confidence: LOW-MEDIUM.                                            |
   |                                                                               |
   | GCN POSITION: Kaak Nakach = highest butter-to-flour shortcrust in dataset      |
   | (2:1 fat ratio); starch node: Lipid_Coated_Starch shared with Makroud(34) and  |
   | Tcharak(35) shortcrust subgroup — but at higher fat loading; K⁺_DatePaste      |
   | node shared with Bradj(49) and Makroud(34); Linalool_GABA node shared with    |
   | Bradj/Makroud/Tcharak/Baklawa (6th dish in floral-water subgroup); NaHCO₃     |
   | node is DATASET-UNIQUE within shortcrust cookies (distinct from Hommos         |
   | NaHCO₃ cooking soda and Baghrir/Matlouh yeast-CO₂ nodes). UNIQUE DISTINCTION: |
   | only baked shortcrust cookie combining chemical leavening + date paste + orange |
   | blossom water in one entry. No smen component (unlike Tcharak/Makroud/Bradj)  |
   | — pure butter shortcrust = different lipid profile (no butyrate SCFA signal). |

   | Interaction [1:1 Fiber-Starch Matrix / Sustained Energy + Lowest GI in        |
   | Tamina Family]: The defining feature of Taminat l-Ghars vs Tamina (37) is     |
   | the 1:1 semolina-to-date-paste ratio. Mixing hot toasted semolina directly    |
   | into an equal mass of soft Ghers (date paste) while both components are hot   |
   | creates a heat-induced fusion of pyrodextrin chains with date insoluble fiber  |
   | (cellulose + pectin). The resulting matrix is physically denser than either    |
   | component alone -- digestive enzymes (alpha-amylase, sucrase) must penetrate  |
   | a fiber-reinforced starch framework rather than pure starch or isolated fiber. |
   | Glycaemic velocity: LOWEST in the semolina subgroup of this dataset --         |
   | below Tamina(37), Makroud(34), and Ftir(48). Corresponds to traditional use   |
   | as sustained-energy postpartum/wedding food. Tmax for co-administered oral    |
   | medications is extended: the dense matrix delays gastric emptying. GCN:        |
   | Fiber_Starch_Matrix = Sustained_Energy node (DATASET-UNIQUE: only dish with  |
   | 1:1 fiber-starch hot-fusion). Confidence: HIGH.                               |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [1kg Ghers / Extreme K+ Load -- Highest Absolute K+ in Dataset]: |
   | Ghers (compressed date paste) provides approximately 700mg K+/100g. At 1kg   |
   | total recipe quantity, the K+ load is ~7000mg across the full preparation.    |
   | Even accounting for individual serving sizes (a typical portion = 150-200g of  |
   | finished product), patients consuming multiple servings at a wedding or         |
   | postpartum celebration easily reach clinically significant K+ intake. For      |
   | patients on ACE inhibitors (K+ retention via aldosterone blockade) or K+-      |
   | sparing diuretics (Spironolactone, Amiloride, Triamterene): severe hyperkalemia|
   | risk. For CKD patients with reduced renal K+ clearance: HIGH-RISK flag --      |
   | this dish represents a CONTRAINDICATION at standard portions. GCN:             |
   | K+_DatePaste_1kg = HIGHEST absolute K+ node in dataset (surpasses Bradj 49,  |
   | Makroud 34, Kaak Nakach 52 which all use date paste as filling, not 1:1 mix). |
   | Confidence: HIGH.                                                             |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Butter 250g Hot-Grain Encapsulation / Lipophilic Drug Cmax        |
   | + Reduced Insulin Spike]: In Taminat l-Ghars the butter is added to the HOT  |
   | toasted semolina first -- before the dates and honey. This means each           |
   | pyrodextrin grain is individually coated in a lipid shell before the fiber     |
   | matrix forms. The double-barrier effect (grain-level lipid coat + date fiber  |
   | wrapping) creates the most effective sugar-release retardation mechanism in    |
   | the Tamina family. This positions Taminat l-Ghars as metabolically "heavier"  |
   | but "safer" glycaemically than Tamina(37). Bile secretion from 250g butter    |
   | amplifies Cmax for all co-ingested lipophilic drugs (Cyclosporine, Isotretinoin|
   | fat-soluble statins, Amiodarone). Unlike Tamina(37), there are no sesame       |
   | lignans -- no CYP2D6 inhibition signal. GCN: Butter_Grain_Encapsulation       |

   | Interaction [Honey ~200g / Mild Biphasic Glucose on Slow Base]:               |

   | Interaction [Sha'ra Strands / High-Surface Bimodal Glucose]: Fine vermicelli  |
   | dough strands (شعرة -- hair-thin threads) have the highest surface-area-to-   |
   | volume ratio of any pastry dough in this dataset. Even with a Smen coating,   |
   | the porous, open-weave structure allows alpha-amylase to access the starch     |
   | matrix faster than the dense pressed layers of Baklawa(33) or the compact     |
   | shortcrust of Tcharak(35). This produces a bimodal glucose profile: a faster   |
   | initial glucose rise from rapid starch hydrolysis in the strands, followed by  |
   | a slower sustained plateau from the walnut-protein matrix slowing nut-absorbed |
   | glucose. GCN: Sha'ra_Strands -- AlphaAmylase_Accessibility = HIGH-SURFACE     |
   | weight; BIMODAL glucose edge -- DATASET-UNIQUE pastry structure node.          |
   | Confidence: HIGH.                                                             |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Walnuts 600g / ALA Antiplatelet + Ellagitannin Endothelial]:      |
   | At 600g, this is the highest single-fill walnut quantity in the dataset        |
   | (Bradj 49 and Baklawa 33 use walnuts but as a sub-component). ALA (alpha-     |
   | linolenic acid, the dominant omega-3 in walnuts) inhibits TXA2 synthesis --   |
   | reducing platelet aggregation. This is additive with Aspirin (COX-1 blockade) |
   | and Clopidogrel (ADP pathway). Walnut skin Ellagitannins are converted by gut  |

   | Anti_Inflammatory_Markers (Positive_Modifier) + Platelet_Inhibition edge.     |
   | HIGHEST walnut dose in dataset. Confidence: MEDIUM-HIGH.                      |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Triple-Phase Smen / Maximum Lipid Microenvironment --             |
   | Pharmacokinetic Boost]: Smen is integrated in three distinct stages: (1) to   |
   | open and separate the dough strands during assembly (strands coated from       |
   | inside); (2) to grease the baking pan (base coat); (3) final top-pour after   |
   | baking over the hot pastry. No other dish in this dataset uses a three-stage  |
   | fat application. The result is a lipid-rich microenvironment at every layer   |
   | level -- the gut encounters a sustained, multi-bolus bile-stimulating lipid    |
   | load throughout digestion. Cmax amplification for lipophilic drugs             |
   | (Cyclosporine, Isotretinoin, Vitamin D3, fat-soluble statins, Amiodarone) is  |

   | Triple_Smen_Application (DATASET-UNIQUE application method). Confidence: HIGH.|
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Triple-Soak Honey + Orange Blossom / Osmotic Load + Linalool]:   |
   | The instruction to soak the hot pastry with syrup, honey, and a full cup of   |

   | Interaction [Bessess + Deep-Fry / Double-Lipid Profile -- Maximum Cmax]:      |
   | Griwech is the only dish in this dataset that combines internal Smen fat       |
   | (bessess dough -- Smen worked into flour at 4:1 ratio, coating every starch   |
   | granule from within) AND external deep-fry oil (applied AFTER shaping).       |
   | The internal butyrate/SFA from Smen triggers the initial bile secretion        |
   | response; the external frying oil (C18:1/C18:2 at high temperature, producing |
   | lipid peroxide byphroducts) adds a second lipid bolus plus oxidised products. |
   | For drugs requiring a high-fat meal for optimal absorption -- Griseofulvin,    |
   | Fenofibrate, Isotretinoin, Cyclosporine, fat-soluble statins -- Griwech acts  |
   | as a pharmacological catalyst delivering MAXIMUM bile stimulation. Caloric     |
   | density is the highest of any fried pastry in the dataset. GCN: Double-Lipid  |
   | flag -- Strong_Positive_Vector for lipophilic drug Cmax. Confidence: HIGH.    |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Braided Lattice + 3mm Thin Dough / Rapid Multi-Directional       |
   | Digestion -- Faster Spike than Baked Shortcrust]: The braiding technique       |
   | (شكل ظفيرة) creates a lattice with exposed dough surfaces on multiple planes.  |
   | Unlike Makroud or Tcharak (solid compact or shortcrust blocks), digestive      |
   | enzymes attack Griwech dough from multiple angles simultaneously. Combined     |
   | with the thin 3mm profile (fully penetrated by honey soak), this means the    |
   | honey-saturated starch matrix is rapidly hydrolysed. Glucose rise is FASTER   |
   | than Tcharak(35) despite similar dough ingredients, because the braiding       |
   | surface and thinness eliminate the fat-barrier delay that shortcrust structure |
   | provides. GCN: Griwech_Structure -- Digestion_Velocity (HIGH); Multi-          |
   | Directional_Enzyme_Access = DATASET-UNIQUE structural node.  Confidence: HIGH.|
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Maillard-Honey Synergy / AGE Concentration + CYP2E1 Load]:       |
   | Frying thin dough to golden-red produces a dense Maillard crust (protein-     |
   | sugar condensation products -- melanoidins + early-stage AGEs). When the hot  |
   | crispy pastry is dipped in warm honey, the sugar solution is trapped within   |
   | the rigid Maillard matrix on cooling, creating a concentrated AGE-sugar pocket.|
   | Hepatic processing of fried lipid peroxides (4-HNE, MDA from hot oil) adds   |
   | CYP2E1 demand -- relevant for patients on Paracetamol (shared CYP2E1          |
   | competition) or on Statins (combined hepatotoxicity signal at high intake).    |
   | GCN: Maillard_AGE_Node + LipidPeroxide_Node -- CYP2E1_Demand; AGE delivery   |
   | per piece is HIGHER than plain-fried dishes due to honey entrapment. Shared   |
   | node class with Brik(43), Bourak(28), Mbessas(47). Confidence: MEDIUM.        |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Sesame Jeljelan / Phytoestrogen Activity + Vitamin E             |
   | Competition]: Whole sesame seeds used as topping deliver sesamin and            |
   | sesamolin at seed-whole level -- lighter dose than the concentrated sesame     |
   | paste (Shamia) in Tamina(37), but pharmacologically active. Sesamin competes  |
   | with Vitamin E (alpha-tocopherol) for CYP3A4 metabolism, potentially          |
   | elevating Vitamin E bioavailability. Sesamolin has mild ERbeta agonism          |
   | (phytoestrogen) -- additive with HRT and mildly antagonistic to Tamoxifen.    |
   | Warfarin: sesamin CYP2D6 mild interference at whole-seed quantities is LOW    |
   | significance per piece. GCN: Jeljelan -- Phytoestrogen_Activity (ERbeta) +    |
   | VitE_Metabolism_Competition. Confidence: LOW-MEDIUM per piece; cumulative    |
   | at large celebration servings: MEDIUM.                                        |
   |                                                                               |
   | GCN POSITION: Griwech = DOUBLE-LIPID node (DATASET-UNIQUE: only dish with    |
   | internal bessess + external deep-fry simultaneously); Braided_Lattice =        |
   | Multi-Directional_Enzyme_Access (structural UNIQUE -- faster glycaemic than   |
   | shortcrust subgroup); Maillard_Honey_Synergy = concentrated AGE-sugar          |
   | entrapment per piece (HIGHER AGE density than plain-fried); Jeljelan_Sesamin  |

   | Interaction [Dioul High-Porosity Structure / Fastest Glucose Entry in         |
   | Fried-Pastry Family]: Dioul (paper-thin dough sheets used in Mhancha) are     |
   | the thinnest laminated pastry dough in this dataset. Deep-frying creates      |
   | micro air-pockets between each gelatinised micro-layer. When hot Mhancha      |
   | rolls are plunged immediately into cold honey, the rapid thermal contraction  |
   | of the air inside creates a vacuum that draws cold honey into every internal  |
   | pocket -- complete saturation of the innermost layers. Alpha-amylase attacks  |
   | the starch from all inter-layer surfaces simultaneously. This produces the     |
   | fastest salivary/pancreatic amylase access of any fried pastry in the dataset:|
   | faster than Griwech braids (multi-directional but thicker), Baklawa pressed   |
   | layers (thicker, baked), and Tcharak (compact shortcrust with fat barrier).   |
   | GCN: Mhancha_Dioul -- Rapid_Glucose_Entry = HIGHEST-porosity starch node;    |
   | ACUTE HYPERGLYCAEMIA flag. Confidence: HIGH.                                  |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [L-Arginine from Peanuts 100g / Minor Vascular NO Buffer]:        |
   | Roasted peanuts contain ~2.4g L-Arginine per 100g -- a meaningful substrate  |
   | dose for endothelial NO synthase (eNOS). As a minor vascular buffer, NO       |
   | production partially counteracts sugar-induced endothelial inflammation.       |
   | For patients on PDE5 inhibitors (Sildenafil, Tadalafil) or high-dose organic |
   | nitrates, the additive vasodilation from 100g peanut L-Arg is lower than      |
   | Shbah el-Safra (200g+ almonds) but still clinically noted. Niacin from        |
   | peanuts mildly supports HDL metabolism. Skinless peanuts used here --         |
   | no PAC/Resveratrol (distinguish from Dziriyet 51 skin-on peanuts). GCN:       |
   | LArg_NO_Vascular = minor buffer edge; SHARED node with Shbah(50), Tcharak(35).|
   | Confidence: MEDIUM.                                                           |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Deep-Fry Oil Saturation / Dioul Fully Oil-Logged -- CCK          |
   | Activation + Cmax Amplification]: Dioul sheets, being paper-thin, absorb      |
   | the maximum proportion of frying oil per unit dough mass of any pastry in     |
   | the dataset. The combination of oil-logged thin sheets + peanut fat triggers  |
   | a strong cholecystokinin (CCK) response -- maximising bile flow. For           |
   | lipophilic medications requiring a high-fat meal (Isotretinoin, Griseofulvin, |
   | Itraconazole, Cyclosporine, fat-soluble statins), Mhancha functions as a      |
   | pharmacological shuttle: Cmax amplification is HIGH. Lipid peroxides (4-HNE,  |
   | MDA) from high-temp oil add a CYP2E1 hepatic demand signal shared with        |
   | Brik(43), Bourak(28), Griwech(55). Orlistat patients: severe GI distress.     |
   | GCN: CCK_Activation -- Cmax-AMPLIFICATION (HIGH); CYP2E1 demand (MEDIUM).    |
   | Confidence: HIGH.                                                             |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Cold Honey 250g Thermal Osmosis / Air-Pocket Vacuum Sugar         |
   | Saturation]: The instruction to transfer Mhancha immediately from hot oil      |
   | into cold honey exploits the same thermodynamic principle as Dziriyet(51) but |
   | through a different physical mechanism: not capillary fill of a porous         |
   | centre, but VACUUM PULL via contracting inter-layer air pockets. The result   |
   | is complete honey saturation of every inter-layer space. 250g honey delivers  |
   | a HIGH fructose+glucose osmotic load. ACUTE HYPERGLYCAEMIA flag for diabetic |
   | patients -- the small physical size of each roll is deceptive; sugar-per-gram  |
   | rivals Dziriyet. NTI osmotic dehydration alert (Lithium/Digoxin). SGLT2        |
   | additive osmotic diuresis. GCN: ThermalOsmosis_AirPocket = DATASET-UNIQUE     |
   | mechanism (distinct from Dziriyet capillary and Zlabia pre-cook pore soak).   |
   | Confidence: HIGH.                                                             |
   |                                                                               |
   | GCN POSITION: Mhancha = HIGHEST-porosity dough node (Dioul = thinnest         |
   | laminated pastry in dataset; faster glycaemic than Griwech lattice and        |
   | Baklawa pressed layers); ThermalOsmosis_AirPocket = DATASET-UNIQUE vacuum     |
   | mechanism (third distinct thermal-osmosis variant after Dziriyet capillary    |
   | and Zlabia wet-pore pre-cook); LArg_NO = SHARED with Shbah(50)/Tcharak(35)   |
   | but at 100g peanut dose (intermediate level); skinless peanut: no PAC node   |
   | (contrast Dziriyet 51); CCK_Activation -- Cmax flag shared with Griwech/      |
   | Shbah/Brik/Bourak fried subgroup; no sesamin (unlike Griwech/Tamina);         |
   | no floral-water Linalool node (unlike most other sweets in dataset).           |
   | RAMADAN/Wedding FLAG: small size causes under-estimation of glycaemic load;   |
   | cumulative multi-piece consumption is HIGH-RISK for diabetic patients.         |
   |                                                                               |

   | Interaction [Indole-3-Carbinol / CYP1A2 Induction -- Estrogen Pathway]:      |
   | Cauliflower glucosinolates are hydrolysed by plant myrosinase (and gut        |
   | bacteria) to yield I3C. Even after boiling (~35% retention), the delivered   |
   | I3C dose is biologically active. I3C induces CYP1A2 and promotes             |
   | 2-hydroxylation of estradiol (2-OHE1 > 16-OHE1), the less proliferative      |
   | metabolite. Clinically relevant for patients on Tamoxifen (altered            |
   | CYP1A2-mediated first-pass) and Aromatase Inhibitors (no adverse but          |
   | pharmacologically interesting). DATASET-UNIQUE: no other dish provides a      |
   | dedicated crucifer I3C node. GCN: I3C_CYP1A2 = DATASET-UNIQUE.              |
   | Confidence: MEDIUM-HIGH.                                                     |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Lycopene + Cooking Oil / Chylomicron Shuttle]:                  |

   | shuttle node with Batata red sauce (58). Confidence: HIGH.                   |

   | Interaction [Pea Amylose+Pectin Gel / Glycaemic Buffer -- Reduced GI vs     |
   | Batata(58)]: Peas provide Amylose (slow-digesting) and Pectin (soluble       |
   | fiber) which form a viscous gel in the small intestine. This gel physically  |
   | traps potato glucose, slowing diffusion to the brush border. Net effect:     |
   | postprandial glucose rise is lower than meatless Batata(58). Saponins from   |
   | peas sequester bile acids, providing mild LDL lowering (synergy with statins)|
   | GCN: Jelbana_Node to Reduced_Glycemic_Velocity. Confidence: HIGH.           |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Piperine 5-Clove-Garlic / Bioavailability Amplifier -- Broad   |
   | CYP3A4 Drug Interaction]: Piperine (white pepper) is a potent inhibitor of  |
   | intestinal and hepatic CYP3A4 and P-glycoprotein. This increases the         |
   | bioavailability of co-administered drugs by reducing first-pass elimination.  |
   | Affected drugs: Propranolol (+55%), Phenytoin, Cyclosporine, Rifampicin,     |
   | Curcumin (+2000%), most CYP3A4 substrates. COMBINED with 5-clove allicin     |

   | Interaction [Acrylamide + Starch-Lipid Complex / Maillard-Fried 1kg Potato  |
   | -- HIGHEST Potato Dose in Dataset]: At deep-fry temperatures (>160C),        |
   | Asparagine and reducing sugars undergo Maillard condensation producing        |
   | Acrylamide (neurotoxic/carcinogenic concern). Simultaneously, amylose helices |
   | complex with fry oil fatty acids forming starch-V complexes -- these slightly |
   | resist amylase but the enormous starch mass (1kg potato) still delivers a    |
   | HIGH glycaemic wave. Acrylamide demands CYP2E1 hepatic detoxification --     |
   | SHARED with Griwech(55), Mhancha(56), Brik(43), Bourak(28). GCN:            |
   | AcrylamideMaillard = CYP2E1_demand (SHARED fried-dish node). HIGH.          |
   | Confidence: HIGH.                                                            |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [4-Egg Choline / Anticholinergic Drug Competition]:              |
   | Four eggs deliver approximately 500mg choline -- well above the Adequate     |
   | Intake of 425-550mg/day. As acetylcholine precursor, this may partially      |
   | counteract anticholinergic drugs (Diphenhydramine, Oxybutynin, Tricyclics,  |
   | Dementia medications). Lutein (fat-soluble xanthophyll) is efficiently        |
   | co-absorbed with the high fry-oil lipid bolus. DATASET-UNIQUE: no other dish |
   | features a dedicated egg Choline+Lutein cognitive node. Confidence: MEDIUM.  |
   |                                                                               |
   | Interaction [Deep-Fry Oil / CCK Activation -- Cmax Amplification for         |
